## 3. Next-step nudge engine specification (Deliverable 3)

### 3.1 Purpose
For a given `(user, project)` compute in ≤ 200ms:
- `phase` — the current 12-phase key (1..12) they are in
- `next_action` — the single most important thing to do next
- `missing` — top-5 unfilled data-room categories or missing SVI-criterion evidence
- `readiness_score` — Investor Readiness (0-100) for their current phase
- `nudge_reason` — human-readable "why we are surfacing this"

### 3.2 Data sources (all cited with path:line)
| Source | Table / Module | Fields used |
|---|---|---|
| Phase state | `startup_phase_progress` (`web/supabase/migrations/0049_startup_phase_progress.sql:6-25`) | phase_id, phase_order, status, completion_pct, steps_json, ai_recommendations |
| Account fast-lookup | `svi_accounts.growth_phase_current` + `growth_completion_pct` (`0049_startup_phase_progress.sql:50-52`) | quick default when no per-phase row yet |
| Project fast-lookup | `projects.growth_phase_current` + `growth_completion_pct` (`:54-57`) | multi-project support |
| Latest SVI score | `svi_analyses` (`web/supabase/migrations/0007_svi_analyses.sql`) | latest per project, dimension scores |
| SVI criterion evidence | `evidence_items` (`web/supabase/migrations/0043_missing_tables.sql:59`) | criterion_key, quality_level, file/link count |
| Data-room file coverage | `dataroom_files` (`web/supabase/migrations/0017_user_source_folders.sql:22`) | folder, doc name, present/absent |
| Founder-pack signals | `founder_packs` (see `web/src/lib/idea-phase/persist.ts:213`) | early-Phase-1 hints |
| Fundraise readiness | `fundraise-checklist.ts:319-347` + `computeReadinessScore` (`:350-370`) | reuse the 70/30 blended score, but per-phase (see P5) |
| Existing SVI next-actions | `svi-analysis.ts:250-1400` (`SVIAnalysis.nextActions`) | fallback next_action when phase-specific rule empty |

### 3.3 Output JSON shape (contract-frozen)
```json
{
  "project_id": "uuid",
  "phase": {
    "key": 5,
    "label_en": "PMF / Early Traction",
    "label_vi": "PMF / Traction ban đầu",
    "canonical_stage": "mvp_early_revenue"
  },
  "next_action": {
    "id": "wire-founder-stripe-testmode",
    "title": "Wire your own Stripe in test-mode",
    "detail": "Chapter 5 CTA — a $1 test charge unlocks the Chapter 7 growth wiring later",
    "surface_route": "/dashboard/integrations",
    "estimated_minutes": 20,
    "priority": "P0"
  },
  "missing": [
    { "id": "cohort-retention-pdf", "category": "traction", "phase_first_needed": 5, "raise_blocker": false, "surface_route": "/dashboard/svi" },
    { "id": "founder-agreement", "category": "corporate", "phase_first_needed": 1, "raise_blocker": true, "surface_route": "/dashboard/data-room" },
    { "id": "ip-assignment-founder", "category": "captable", "phase_first_needed": 1, "raise_blocker": true, "surface_route": "/dashboard/data-room" },
    { "id": "abn-registration", "category": "corporate", "phase_first_needed": 1, "raise_blocker": true, "surface_route": "/dashboard/onboarding" },
    { "id": "pmf-signal", "category": "traction", "phase_first_needed": 5, "raise_blocker": false, "surface_route": "/dashboard/svi" }
  ],
  "readiness_score": {
    "current_phase": 5,
    "score": 42,
    "band": "not-ready",
    "delta_vs_last_week": 6,
    "svi_subset_used": ["idea", "market", "customer_size", "revenue"]
  },
  "nudge_reason": "You've completed 3 of the 6 Chapter 5 milestones. Wiring Stripe test-mode now unlocks Chapter 7 in one click instead of a 2-week project.",
  "meta": {
    "computed_at": "2026-07-24T02:00:00Z",
    "fresh_for_seconds": 3600,
    "used_sources": ["startup_phase_progress", "svi_analyses", "dataroom_files", "evidence_items"],
    "afsl_disclaimer": "General information only, not personal financial product advice under s766B Corporations Act 2001 (Cth)."
  }
}
```

### 3.4 API endpoint
- **Route:** `GET /api/nudge/next-steps?project_id={uuid}` (default: caller's most-recent project).
- **Handler location:** `web/src/app/api/nudge/next-steps/route.ts` (new).
- **Auth:** existing `withAuth` wrapper; RLS scoped to caller's `svi_accounts.id`.
- **Cache:** ETag on `updated_at` of the most-recent `startup_phase_progress` row for the project; `Cache-Control: private, max-age=3600` (matches `fresh_for_seconds`).
- **Errors:** `404` when project unknown; `403` when caller lacks project access; `200 { "state": "not-started", ...zero-payload }` when the founder has no SVI row yet (encourages onboarding rather than erroring).

### 3.5 Surfaces (where the payload renders)
| Surface | What it shows | Refresh trigger |
|---|---|---|
| `/dashboard` tile "Your next step" | phase + next_action + readiness score band | on page load + on `startup_phase_progress` mutation |
| In-app banner (dismissible per-day) | next_action.title + priority chip | on every dashboard nav |
| Weekly digest email (see P7) | readiness score + delta + top-3 missing + single next action | cron Monday 07:00 AEST (or founder-configured, per `email_preferences.ts:232`) |
| SVI report PDF appendix | full JSON dump + rendered checklist | on report generation only |
| `/showcase/atlassian` "you are here" overlay (sibling agent's UI) | phase label + canonical-stage badge | on page load only |

### 3.6 Cadence + freshness rules
- **Event-driven recompute** on any of: `startup_phase_progress.status` transition, `svi_analyses` insert, `dataroom_files` insert/delete, `evidence_items` insert. Fire via Supabase `pg_notify('nudge_refresh', project_id::text)`; a lightweight listener enqueues.
- **Nightly recompute cron** at 02:00 AEST covers projects with no events (kept-warm; freshness for the weekly digest).
- **No email spam:** hard rule — never send more than 1 nudge email per project per 7 days regardless of trigger.
- **Kill switch:** env `NUDGE_ENGINE=off` disables the endpoint + cron.

### 3.7 Fair-use guardrails
- **AFSL boundary:** the `next_action.detail` field NEVER prescribes a specific investment, pricing, or legal decision. It only points to a workspace surface. All personal-advice-shaped output routes to the AFSL disclaimer in `meta.afsl_disclaimer`.
- **Wholesale-investor status inference:** the engine NEVER concludes a user is a wholesale/sophisticated investor. That determination is off-platform (Q2).
- **ESIC eligibility:** the engine surfaces a "run the ESIC self-assessment" nudge but does not itself certify eligibility (P6 gate does the check, still marked "information only").

The sibling agent is authoring a 9-step logged-out walkthrough at `/showcase/atlassian`. This table lists — per step — the Atlassian real-world moment it demonstrates, the BlockID surface a live founder would use at the same journey point, and what the demo needs to make the connection obvious.

| Step | Atlassian real-world moment | BlockID surface for a live founder | Demo callout copy | You-are-here indicator |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 · `/showcase/atlassian` intro | 2002 UNSW grads with A$10K credit card (`page.tsx:37-43`) | `/svi` (idea seed form) + `/guide/01-vision` | "Every founder starts here. Type your one-liner — BlockID's Chapter 1 mirrors what Cannon-Brookes + Farquhar did on a whiteboard, minus the whiteboard." | phase-1 badge |
| 2 · `/dashboard` demo tile | 2003 first US$1M revenue from AA-buys-Jira-via-fax (`page.tsx:52-60`) | `/dashboard` "Your next step" tile (see §3.5) fed by nudge engine | "The green tile is what your workspace looks like once you have any SVI row. Atlassian didn't have a dashboard — imagine if they had." | phase-5 badge |
| 3 · `/svi-report` demo | Pre-2010 profitability + product-led-growth defining ratios (S-1 MD&A) | `/svi` full report + `svi_analyses.report_pdf_url` | "This is a real BlockID SVI report. The 13-criterion breakdown is what turns a founder hunch into an investor-defensible score." | phase-2 badge |
| 4 · `/growth-phases` demo | Ten-year path 2002-2012 (bootstrap → Accel → board scale) | `/guide` chapter grid (12 chapters) | "Atlassian didn't skip phases — they compressed some (no primary VC). The 12-chapter guide shows every phase; you decide which to compress." | phase-4/5/8 sequence |
| 5 · `/agents/[slug]` demo | 2007 Five core values (`page.tsx:86-92`), 2005 ShipIt (`page.tsx:69-76`) | `/dashboard/team` + CHRO agent output | "Your CHRO agent drafts the culture doc BlockID uses internally. Atlassian took years to codify five values; you get a starting draft in minutes." | phase-8 badge |
| 6 · `/data-room` demo | Atlassian S-1 Item 16 exhibit index (102 exhibits mapped in §2 above) | `/dashboard/data-room` 12-folder / 102-item structure | "Every exhibit Atlassian filed with the SEC has a BlockID counterpart. Grey items are what BlockID auto-generates for you." | phase-9 badge |
| 7 · `/valuation` demo | 2014 T. Rowe secondary at US$3.3B (`page.tsx:119-127`) | `/dashboard/valuation` + `/dashboard/cfo` | "SVI-calibrated valuation is a starting anchor, not personal financial advice. The AFSL disclaimer stays visible on every valuation output." | phase-10 badge |
| 8 · `/guide` demo | 2010 Accel 100% secondary + 2015 IPO (`page.tsx:94-146`) | `/guide/10-fundraise` chapter | "Both of Atlassian's pre-IPO rounds were 100% secondary — Chapter 10 shows you how to distinguish primary vs secondary when you get there." | phase-10 badge |
| 9 · `/showcase/atlassian` recap | FY25 US$5.2B revenue, +20% YoY, 83% GM (`page.tsx:200-207`) | `/dashboard/portfolio` (multi-project view) + `/showcase/blockid` (dogfood proof) | "You've seen where a workspace can lead. Start yours at /svi — the reseller link ?via=INFOVISION20 gives you 20% off your first paid tier." | phase-11/12 badge |

## 5. AU statutory reference index (for future auto-loop tasks)

Compact lookup so subsequent ticks don't re-fetch:

| Concept | Statute / Rule | Section | Relevant BlockID phase |
|---|---|---|---|
| Small-scale personal offer exemption | Corporations Act 2001 (Cth) | s708(1) — max 20 investors / A$2M / 12 mo | 9-10 |
| Sophisticated investor test | Corporations Act 2001 (Cth) | s708(8) — A$2.5M net assets or A$250k gross income (2 FYs), needs qualified accountant certificate | 9-10 |
| Professional investor test | Corporations Act 2001 (Cth) | s708(11) — AFS licensee or A$10M+ portfolio | 10 |
| Wholesale client (services) | Corporations Act 2001 (Cth) | s761G(7) | 10-12 |
| Personal advice boundary | Corporations Act 2001 (Cth) | s766B | every SVI/valuation output |
| ESOP tax concessions | ITAA 1997 | Subdiv 83A-B (upfront) / 83A-C (deferred) | 8 |
| ESOP start-up tests | ITAA 1997 | s83A-33 (7 eligibility tests) + s83A-45(4) 10% cap + s83A-45(5) forfeiture / 3-year hold | 8 |
| ESIC 20% offset + 10Y CGT exemption | ITAA 1997 Div 360 | 3 gateway tests + principles or points test | 3-9 |
| ESVCLP fund concessions | Venture Capital Act 2002 (Cth) | ESVCLP registration via AusIndustry | 9-10 |
| R&D Tax Incentive | Industry Research and Development Act 1986 | AusIndustry registration ≤ 10 mo post-FY | 6-11 |
| GST registration threshold | A New Tax System (GST) Act 1999 | A$75k turnover | 6+ |
| Privacy — APPs | Privacy Act 1988 (Cth) | 13 APPs (Schedule 1) | 4+ |
| Notifiable Data Breaches Scheme | Privacy Act 1988 (Cth) | Part IIIC, ≤ 30-day notification | 7+ |
| WGEA reporting | Workplace Gender Equality Act 2012 | ≥ 100 employees | 11-12 |
| Modern slavery statement | Modern Slavery Act 2018 (Cth) | ≥ A$100M consolidated revenue | 11-12 |
| Fair Work NES | Fair Work Act 2009 (Cth) | 11 minimum standards + 121 modern awards | 8+ |
| ASIC annual review | Corporations Act 2001 (Cth) | s345A + regs | 11 annually |

Emits three event kinds inside a 12-month horizon: (1) BAS quarterly lodgment (Q1 due 28-Oct, Q2 due 28-Feb, Q3 due 28-Apr, Q4 due 28-Jul, standard 1-Jul-to-30-Jun FY, self-lodger dates from the ATO BAS due-dates page) gated on `compliance_gst_status.registered_for_gst`, (2) ASIC annual company review (`s 345A Corporations Act 2001 (Cth)`) anchored on the incorporation-date anniversary (reads `equity_plans.incorporation_date` as the closest ACN-registration-date proxy currently persisted; gracefully no-ops when absent or unparseable), (3) AusIndustry R&D Tax Incentive registration deadline (10-month post-FY window, `IR&D Act 1986 s 27A`) fed by the existing `buildRDCalendar()` output so the two surfaces never drift; skips FYs already `registered` / `not_applicable` / overdue. Every VEVENT carries a `DTSTART;VALUE=DATE` all-day block, stable deterministic UID (`bas-fy{yyyy-yy}-q{n}@blockid.au`, `asic-annual-review-{yyyy}@blockid.au`, `rd-registration-fy{yyyy-yy}@blockid.au`), fixed 14-day `VALARM` (`TRIGGER:-P14D`, matches Chapter 11 copy at `web/src/lib/guide/startup-journey.ts:870`), and an inline `URL` back to the authoritative ATO / ASIC / business.gov.au source. RFC 5545 line-folding (75-octet lines, CRLF join, continuation-space prefix) and text-escape (`\,`, `\;`, `\\`, `\n`) implemented in-module. `CALENDAR_DISCLAIMER` fixed on the `X-WR-CALDESC` calendar-level property so subscribers see "not tax or legal advice — BAS due dates assume standard 1-July-to-30-June FY and self-lodgment" every time they refresh. Test coverage in `web/src/lib/compliance/calendar.test.ts` — 14 vitest cases across four describe blocks: (a) BAS quarterly gating (no events when `gst_registered=false`, Q1 due 2026-10-28 with correct summary + UID + reminder, Q2 rolls into next calendar year at 2027-02-28, horizon caps everything before 2027-07-24), (b) ASIC anniversary (no events without ACN date, 2022-08-04 registration → 2026-08-04 event with `s 345A` grep, unparseable date gracefully skipped), (c) R&D deadline (skips `registered`, skips `not_applicable`, skips overdue, emits FY 2025-26 event on 2027-04-30 with `IR&D Act 1986 s 27A` grep + stable UID), (d) `renderIcs` RFC 5545 shape (starts/ends with `BEGIN`/`END:VCALENDAR`, fixed PRODID, `X-WR-CALDESC` present + fragment guard, one CRLF-terminated `BEGIN:VALARM` per event with `TRIGGER:-P14D` + `ACTION:DISPLAY`, deterministic UIDs across two calls). All 64 tests in `src/lib/compliance/` green (50 existing + 14 new). Closes §1 phase 11 P1 gap (compliance-calendar.ics generator missing) and delivers the reminder half of §1 phase 6 P1 gap "AusIndustry registration-deadline (10 months post-FY-end) alert wired to compliance-calendar" flagged by P1j. Founder still needs to enter incorporation_date (equity_plans row) for the ASIC branch to fire; the P1n follow-up will fold in WGEA + Modern Slavery threshold events once those detectors ship.

- **P1n** WGEA + Modern Slavery threshold detectors (auto-fire on headcount / revenue crossings) — **shipped 2026-07-24**. Two pure detectors added under `web/src/lib/compliance/`, mirroring the P1i `gst-threshold.ts` shape so downstream cron / dashboard-panel wiring can reuse the same result-envelope contract.
  - `wgea-threshold.ts` — `assessWGEA(input, now)` + `assessWGEAFromHeadcount(headcount, opts)` shortcut for callers that only know a current Australian headcount. Anchors on **Workplace Gender Equality Act 2012 (Cth) s3** (100-employee "relevant employer" definition) and **s3B(2)** (2-reporting-period grace once you drop below 100). Five-branch matrix: `not_required` (< 80) / `approaching_threshold` (80–99 warning band) / `report_required` (peak-in-trailing-12mo ≥ 100 — uses peak, not current, so a mid-year hire spike still fires) / `grace_period` (previously reported but now below 100, remaining periods surfaced in the reasoning string) / `already_reporting` (fires when `last_report_lodged_at` set + still a relevant employer). Also computes `next_report_due_iso` — the WGEA public-report deadline is 31-May immediately after the 1-Apr → 31-Mar reporting period, rolls to next year once today is past 31-May. `WGEA_DISCLAIMER` fixed on every result so the surface can never render the classification without the "confirm with WGEA helpline / registered consultant" hedge (avoids s923B / s911A Corps Act exposure).
  - `modern-slavery-threshold.ts` — `assessModernSlavery(input, now)` + `assessModernSlaveryFromRevenue(revenueAud, opts)` shortcut. Anchors on **Modern Slavery Act 2018 (Cth) s5** (A$100M consolidated-revenue threshold + Australian-entity-or-carrying-on-business-in-Australia scope), **s13** (statement due within 6 months of FY end, governing-body approval + responsible-member signature required), **s16** (7 mandatory reporting criteria), and **s6** (voluntary opt-in for entities below the threshold — surfaced in the approaching_threshold reasoning string as an acquirer-signal opportunity). Six-branch matrix: `not_required` (< 80M or foreign-entity-not-carrying-on-business-in-AU) / `approaching_threshold` (A$80M–99M warning band) / `statement_required` (projected current-period ≥ A$100M — pre-FY-end, warning) / `statement_due_soon` (last-FY ≥ A$100M + FY has ended + within 6-month window, critical) / `statement_overdue` (window has passed, critical + days-overdue count) / `already_lodged` (fires when `last_statement_lodged_at` set + last FY was above threshold). Also computes `statement_due_iso` (FY end + 6 months, clamped to end-of-target-month so 31-Dec-2026 → 30-Jun-2027 not 1-Jul-2027) and `days_until_statement_due` (signed integer, negative when overdue). `MODERN_SLAVERY_DISCLAIMER` cites AASB 10 controlled-entity aggregation so callers know "consolidated revenue" is not just the parent-entity P&L.
  - Test coverage — `wgea-threshold.test.ts` (13 vitest cases across 4 describe blocks: branch matrix incl. peak-headcount uses history not just current, grace-period-exit after WGEA_GRACE_PERIODS elapse, NaN / negative / non-numeric-string tolerance, shortcut behaviour incl. lodged-report roundtrip, deadline-rolls-year-boundary at 31-May, constants pin) + `modern-slavery-threshold.test.ts` (16 vitest cases across 4 describe blocks: branch matrix incl. projected-vs-last-FY split, statement_due_soon vs statement_overdue day-count sign, foreign-entity-not-carrying-on-business exit, NaN / negative tolerance, shortcut behaviour incl. carrying-on-business flag propagation, 30-Jun and 31-Dec FY-end 6-month-window arithmetic, constants pin). All 93 `src/lib/compliance/` tests green (64 existing + 29 new).
  - Wire-in path — the two `assess*` helpers are pure (no I/O, no imports besides types) and take a `now` arg, so a future `/api/compliance/wgea-threshold` + `/api/compliance/modern-slavery-threshold` route can reuse the exact pattern `web/src/app/api/compliance/gst-threshold/route.ts` established (auth-gated POST that persists to a new `compliance_wgea_status` / `compliance_modern_slavery_status` table alongside `compliance_gst_status`), and the existing `web/src/components/dashboard/compliance-panel.tsx` tile can grow two more rows keyed off the same result-envelope shape. That schema migration + dashboard-panel wiring is a separate tick (P1n-persist) — this tick ships the detector primitives only, closing **§1 phase 11 "WGEA + Modern Slavery threshold detectors (currently upload-only in data-room, no automated headcount/turnover trigger)"** and **§2 folder 12 items 12.5 (WGEA Compliance Report — was NUDGE with headcount trigger missing) and 12.6 (Modern Slavery Act Statement — was NUDGE with revenue trigger missing)**.

### Round 6 follow-ups (spun off from P5 / P6 / P7)

- **P5a** true readiness_by_phase(input) helper — **shipped 2026-07-24**. Pure module `web/src/lib/nudge/readiness-by-phase.ts` exports `computeReadinessByPhase({ sviScores, dataroomRows }) → Record<PhaseKey, { score, band: "not-ready"|"warming-up"|"investor-ready", missing_top3: NudgeMissingItem[], criteria_used: CriterionKey[] }>`. Each of the 12 phases picks its own `CriterionKey` subset via `PHASE_CRITERION_SUBSET` (derived from §1 of this goal — e.g. phase 3 = market + customer_size + gtm_strategy, phase 9 = documents + dataroom + revenue + team + gtm_strategy, phase 12 = revenue + documents + team_structure + dataroom) and scores it as a weighted average using `evaluation-criteria.ts` CRITERIA[i].weight so the readiness signal stays consistent with the composite enhanced SVI score. Missing artefacts are phase-scoped (only rows with `phaseSlug ≤ phase`), filter out any `dataroom_files` row whose status is not `missing`/`archived`, and sort raise-blockers-first — the top 3 land in `missing_top3`. Bands mirror the existing tile UX: ≥75 investor-ready, ≥50 warming-up, else not-ready. Wired into `computeNextSteps` via a new `readiness_by_phase: Record<string, PhaseReadinessEntry>` field on `NudgeResult` (stringified PhaseKey ordinals so JSON round-trips cleanly through fetch on the client); the API route now surfaces it under `body.result.readiness_by_phase` on the existing `/api/nudge/next-steps` endpoint (no separate route). Test coverage in `web/src/lib/nudge/readiness-by-phase.test.ts` — 8 vitest cases pinning (a) 12-phase completeness + not-ready floor, (b) every subset only cites known `CRITERION_KEYS`, (c) weighted-average arithmetic (phase 3 with market/customer_size/gtm_strategy = 90/60/30 lands on 64 → warming-up), (d) band thresholds (all-80 → investor-ready across every phase, all-55 → warming-up), (e) dimension-key fallback + 0..1 fractional score normalisation, (f) missing_top3 phase scope + raise-blockers-first ordering, (g) present-dataroom-row filtering, (h) NaN / clamp / negative tolerance. All 8 tests green. Sibling tests still pass (20 tests across nudge + tile helpers). Typecheck green. Tile-side per-phase view (switching InvestorReadinessTile to consume `readiness_by_phase[currentPhase]` instead of the blended sub_scores tuple) remains a follow-up under P5a-tile — this tick ships the engine + API surface; the visual switch is a self-contained tile refactor that carries its own Playwright spec update.

- **P6a** Div 83A ESOP scheme-rules gate — **shipped 2026-07-24**. Two-file split mirroring the ESIC gate: (1) `web/src/lib/compliance/div83a-qualifying-tests.ts` exports the 8-point ATO start-up concession checklist as a typed fixture (`DIV83A_QUALIFYING_TESTS: readonly Div83AQualifyingTest[]` — each row carries `{key, section, en, vi}` with EN + VI parity + a stable ITAA-1997 anchor per test) plus `div83aQualifyingTestsFor(locale)` accessor and `DIV83A_QUALIFYING_TESTS_DISCLAIMER` — the fixture is the source-of-truth copy previously duplicated inside Chapter 8 body (`web/src/lib/guide/startup-journey.ts:662-682`) so future ticks can import it instead of re-declaring. (2) `web/src/lib/compliance/div83a-funding-gate.ts` exports `assertDiv83AEligibleOrWarn(supabase, {userId, projectId, requireEligible, action})` mirroring the `assertESICEligibleOrWarn()` shape exactly — reads `esop_option_grants` (migration `0089_esop_grants.sql`) scoped to `(user_id, project_id, status='active')`, applies a worst-first precedence over the branch matrix (`grants_ineligible` → `grants_unsure` → `unchecked_grants` → `check_stale` → pass), and returns the same `Div83AGateOk | Div83AGateFail` envelope shape as the ESIC gate. Six reasons: `no_grants_on_file` (pass-through — a round without ESOP grants cannot mislead an employee about Div 83A), `unchecked_grants` (grant with `div83a_status = NULL`), `check_stale` (`div83a_last_checked_at > 180 days`; stale-after horizon deliberately longer than the ESIC 90-day window because grant terms rarely change between checks — the check only re-fails when the *company* crosses the s83A-33(1)(a) A$50m turnover cap or the s83A-33(1)(c) 10-year age cap), `grants_unsure`, `grants_ineligible`, and `db_unavailable`. Warn-only by default (attaches `div83a_warn` envelope to the fundraise 200 response + emits `div83a_gate.warn_*` analytics); returns 412 when body.wholesaleOnly=true so wholesale-only rounds that pitch the ESS start-up concession cannot ship while an active grant fails. JSDoc cites ITAA97 Subdiv 83A-B/83A-C, ATO ESS start-up concession guidance, and Corps Act s911A / s1041H exposure. Wired into `web/src/app/api/fundraise/route.ts` immediately after the ESIC gate — same `try` block, same `getActiveProject(user.id)` lookup, same 412-on-block / warn-envelope-on-pass semantics; happy-path 200 response now carries `esic_warn?` AND `div83a_warn?` when either fires. Test coverage: `div83a-qualifying-tests.test.ts` (5 vitest cases pinning 8-row EN/VI parity, unique keys, s83A statutory anchor grep, verbatim match against Chapter 8 body copy via `getChapter("08-team")`, and disclaimer presence) + `div83a-funding-gate.test.ts` (15 vitest cases across 2 describe blocks pinning both warn-only and blocking-mode branch matrices — empty-grants pass, all-eligible-fresh pass, unchecked warn/block, stale warn/block, unsure warn/block, ineligible warn/block with precedence over unchecked+stale, db-null and query-error both surface as `db_unavailable`). All 113 `src/lib/compliance/` tests green (93 existing + 20 new). Typecheck `npx tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json` green. Closes P6 exit-criterion #2 ("all 8 Div83A tests already in startup-journey.ts:621-641 exposed via a shared module and wired into ch09 investor-readiness pack"). - **P7b** persist last-week readiness snapshot per project — **shipped 2026-07-24**. Delivers the baseline table + pure snapshot helpers so P7a's founder-side weekly digest can compute a week-over-week readiness delta without recomputing every historic tick. Migration `web/supabase/migrations/0113_svi_readiness_snapshots.sql` — table `svi_readiness_snapshots(id, user_id, project_id, phase_slug, overall_score, band, readiness_by_phase, missing_top3, next_action_title, source, computed_at)` mirroring the `compliance_gst_status` shape from 0108: default-deny RLS, `(user_id, project_id, computed_at DESC)` composite index for the "latest snapshot per founder-project" read + a secondary `computed_at DESC` index for the cron-side "scan-recent-snapshots" pattern the P7a cron will use to page through founders. `overall_score` guarded by a `[0,100]` CHECK, `band` guarded by a 3-value CHECK (`not-ready`/`warming-up`/`investor-ready`), `source` guarded by a 4-value CHECK (`nudge_engine`/`manual`/`cron`/`digest`) so the read side can filter to just the digest-eligible rows. Applied via the pinned docker-exec-psql-then-NOTIFY-pgrst recipe (reference_db_migrations memory — manual apply, never auto on deploy). Pure module `web/src/lib/nudge/readiness-snapshots.ts` exports (1) `toSnapshotRow(nudgeResult, {userId, projectId, source?})` — projects the `NudgeResult` from `computeNextSteps()` into the wire shape, clamps `readiness_score.overall` to `[0,100]`, derives the `band` from the clamped score using the same ≥75/≥50/else thresholds as the tile UX contract, keeps `project_id` null when a founder has no active project, and defaults `source` to `nudge_engine` so the /api/nudge/next-steps route can persist a snapshot on every fetch without a source arg; (2) `computeReadinessDelta(current, previous)` — pure delta helper returning `{score_delta, summary, band_direction, previous_computed_at}` covering four branches — first-snapshot (previous=null → `"First readiness snapshot"` summary + delta 0), same band + non-zero delta (renders `+N points up` / `−N points down`), band-up crossing (`crossed a band boundary upward` summary), band-down crossing (`slipped into a lower readiness band`); tolerates NaN / non-finite scores on the previous row so a corrupted historic snapshot cannot crash the digest render; (3) thin I/O helpers `writeReadinessSnapshot(sb, row)` (single-row insert returning `{id, computed_at}` or null on error) and `fetchLatestReadinessSnapshot(sb, {userId, projectId, before?})` (most-recent-first read; the optional `before` cutoff is what P7a will use to fetch the pre-digest baseline while writing the fresh snapshot in the same run). Test coverage `web/src/lib/nudge/readiness-snapshots.test.ts` — 12 vitest cases across two describe blocks: `toSnapshotRow` (6 cases pinning wire-shape projection + missing_top3 truncation to 3 + project_id null handling + score-clamp-and-band-derive for both overshoot (999→100 band investor-ready) and NaN/negative (→0 band not-ready) + source override for cron/digest/manual + empty-next-action tolerance); `computeReadinessDelta` (6 cases pinning first-snapshot summary + positive delta same-band summary + band-up crossing summary + band-down crossing summary with negative delta + no-change summary + NaN-previous-score tolerance so `Number.isFinite(delta.score_delta)` holds). All 12 tests green. Typecheck `npx tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json` green. Closes P7b entirely — the founder-side weekly digest cron (P7a) can now call `computeNextSteps()` → `toSnapshotRow()` → `writeReadinessSnapshot()` to persist the week's snapshot, then `fetchLatestReadinessSnapshot(..., {before: nowIso})` to grab the previous week's baseline, then `computeReadinessDelta()` to render the "Your investor readiness this week" digest section without a schema change on `startup_phase_progress`.

interviewee identity table with a **compensation_offered** row + explicit "if anything other than 'None', disclose that compensation alongside every quote used in investor materials" guardrail (the New template body at `web/content/templates/legal/au-customer-loi.md` — 12-section markdown covering (1) purpose + authority-to-sign rep, (2) intended use case + stakeholders, (3) indicative-commercial-terms table (ACV / seats / start-date / payment / GST-exclusive with s29-70 GST-tax-invoice anchor), (4) conditions to Definitive Agreement (technical PoV against Annexure A + APP privacy + budget approval + statutory approvals), (5) BINDING confidentiality clause with explicit "paragraphs 5.2 / 8 / 9 / 10 / 11 are binding, everything else is non-binding present-intention only" split, (6) non-binding-nature clause naming the s18 Australian Consumer Law + s1041H Corporations Act 2001 (Cth) misleading-conduct anchors so the founder cannot accidentally overstate the LOI as committed revenue in a data room (also explicitly permits the Supplier to disclose the LOI to prospective investors + advisers + lenders provided the non-binding nature is stated), (7) Definitive Agreement target date, (8) non-binding confirmation, (9) publicity (customer name may be disclosed to investors under NDA in aggregate form but not on public marketing without consent), (10) costs (each party bears own), (11) governing-law + ADC-mediation-first dispute path, (12) Electronic Transactions Act 1999 (Cth) e-signing anchor. Signatures block executes under s127 Corporations Act 2001 (Cth) with both a `{{#second_signatory}}` co-signatory toggle AND a `{{#sole_director}}` s204A(1) sole-director-and-sole-company-secretary toggle so single-founder Pty Ltds can sign without inventing a second officer. Two Annexures (A acceptance criteria + B statutory approvals) keep the raise-specific detail out of the main body. New category `commercial` on the `LegalTemplateCategory` union (previously `corporate | employment | fundraising | ip` — LOI is neither legal-instrument nor fundraise-security, it is a commercial letter, so the union grows by one). Registry entry in `web/src/lib/templates/legal-templates.ts` — slug `au-customer-loi`, `category: "commercial"`, `phase_slug: "phase-2"`, 24 declared placeholders (customer identity + supplier identity + product + commercial figures + acceptance criteria + statutory approvals + revision date + both signatory-toggle variants), 6 source citations (ACL s18 + Corps Act s1041H/s769C/s127 + APPs + GST Act s29-70 + Electronic Transactions Act + ADC Commercial Mediation Guidelines). Test coverage `web/src/lib/templates/legal-templates.test.ts` gains 4 vitest cases mirroring the P1c / P1a shape: (1) is registered as phase-2 commercial template, (2) declares the AU misleading-conduct anchors an investor's lawyer looks for (s18 ACL + Australian Consumer Law + s1041H + non-binding + Confidentiality + s127 + s29-70 + Electronic Transactions Act 1999 + NOT LEGAL ADVICE + legally-binding-language split all grepped), (3) declares every non-toggle `{{token}}` in the body is present in `placeholders[]`, (4) substitutes party + commercial fields and honours signatory toggles — co-signatory variant keeps Bob Founder + strips sole-director block; sole-director variant keeps sole-director-and-sole-company-secretary line + strips second_signatory_name; section-toggle tokens never leak into rendered output. Registry-level whitelist test in `LEGAL_TEMPLATES registry` describe block updated from `["corporate", "employment", "fundraising", "ip"]` to include `"commercial"`. All 30 legal-template tests green (26 existing + 4 new). Founder still must confirm registered office + ACN + first-director details in the Signatures block; template hard-codes the "non-binding except confidentiality + publicity + governing-law" split so a mis-configured LOI cannot silently drift into a binding purchase order. Closes §2 folder 8 item "Letter of Intent (customer)" — previously NO-TEMPLATE, now NUDGE with default body + investor-safe disclosure clause.

- **P1k-view** founder-facing `/compliance/calendar` page — **shipped 2026-07-25**. Closes the last UI leg of the P1k / P1n-calendar-events chain. The pure `buildComplianceCalendar()` module (P1k) + WGEA/Modern-Slavery event kinds (P1n-calendar-events) + `GET /api/compliance/calendar` route have shipped since 2026-07-24; until this tick the founder had to know the API URL by heart. New server RSC at `web/src/app/compliance/calendar/page.tsx` — auth-gated (`redirect("/auth/login?next=/compliance/calendar")`), `dynamic = "force-dynamic"`, `robots: { index: false, follow: false }`. Loads the same 5 sources as the `.ics` route (`compliance_gst_status.registered_for_gst`, `equity_plans.incorporation_date`, `compliance_rd_registrations`, latest `compliance_wgea_status.result_json`, latest `compliance_modern_slavery_status.result_json`) via one `Promise.all`, threads them into `buildRDCalendar()` then `buildComplianceCalendar()` — same data pipeline the API route uses so the two surfaces cannot drift. Client component `calendar-view-client.tsx` renders (a) subscribe controls (`Download .ics` link at `/api/compliance/calendar?download=1`, `webcal://` one-click subscribe button, https:// fallback URL with copy-to-clipboard button — falls back to `window.prompt` on non-secure contexts / older browsers so a founder inside a corporate VPN still gets the URL), (b) a `Next up` callout tile keyed on `pickNextEvent(events, now)` with band-coloured Icon + `days until / X days overdue` phrase + deep-links to both the in-app guide (`EVENT_KIND_HELP_ROUTE`) and the authoritative source (`event.source_url`), (c) the full event list grouped by month via `groupByMonth(events)` with band-coloured rows + `Guide →` deep-link per row + `EVENT_KIND_LABEL` chip, and (d) the fixed `CALENDAR_DISCLAIMER` (BAS due dates assume standard 1-Jul-to-30-Jun FY + self-lodgment). Pure helpers `calendar-view.helpers.ts` — `EVENT_KIND_LABEL` / `EVENT_KIND_HELP_ROUTE` label + route packs keyed on every `ComplianceEventKind`, `daysUntil(iso, now) → integer` (UTC day-diff, NaN-safe on unparseable input), `pickCalendarBand(event, now) → "green"|"amber"|"red"` (red overdue / amber ≤14 days / green further / unparseable falls back to green so a corrupted row does not scream at the founder), `formatDueDate(iso) → "28 Oct 2026 (Wed)"` (weekday abbreviation so the founder does not need a calendar app to know the day; falls back to raw ISO on unparseable), `groupByMonth(events) → CalendarMonthGroup[]` (chronological year-month bucketing, sorted "unknown" bucket last for corrupted dates), `pickNextEvent(events, now)` (prefers the soonest non-overdue event; falls back to the earliest overdue event when everything has passed; ties broken by summary alphabetical for stable render output), `buildSubscribeUrl(base) → { webcal, https }` (webcal:// prefix triggers the one-click subscribe on Google Calendar / iCal / Outlook; https:// preserved for tools that only accept https feed URLs; empty base defaults to `https://blockid.au`, bare hostname gets `https://` prepended, existing http:// preserved for local dev). Test coverage `calendar-view.helpers.test.ts` — 24 vitest cases across 8 describe blocks: label + route completeness for every `ComplianceEventKind`; `daysUntil` (today = 0, future positive, past negative, NaN on unparseable); `pickCalendarBand` branch matrix (overdue → red, ≤14 days → amber, further → green, unparseable → green); `formatDueDate` (Oct 2026 Wed / Feb 2027 Sun anchor + raw fallback); `groupByMonth` (chronological bucket sort, empty-input, "unknown" bucket routing for unparseable dates); `pickNextEvent` (soonest future preferred, prefers future over overdue, falls back to earliest overdue when nothing future, empty → null, tie-break by summary alphabetical); `buildSubscribeUrl` (bare hostname gets https:// prepended, trailing slash stripped, http:// preserved for local dev, empty base defaults to blockid.au). All 24 tests green. `data-testid` attributes on subscribe controls (`calendar-download-link`, `calendar-webcal-link`, `calendar-copy-https`, `calendar-https-url`), `Next up` tile (`calendar-next-up` + `data-band`), event list (`calendar-event-list`, `calendar-event-row` + `data-kind` + `data-band`), and empty state (`calendar-empty-state`) so a future Playwright spec can attach without helper edits. Typecheck `npx tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json` green (unrelated pre-existing `src/components/i18n/translation-provider.tsx` diagnostics — not touched by this tick). Closes the founder-facing view leg of P1k. Nav-link + `/dashboard` tile pointer are natural follow-ups (P1k-nav) — the page is discoverable today via the existing `/compliance/gst`, `/compliance/wgea`, `/compliance/modern-slavery`, `/compliance/rd` deep-links each pointing back into `/compliance/calendar` via the `EVENT_KIND_HELP_ROUTE` map, but a top-level sidebar entry would remove the two-hop.

### Round 14 follow-ups (spun off from P3-ibisworld-deeplinks — §1 phase 3 P1 founder-facing surface)

- **P3-ibisworld-tile-wire** wire `findIbisworldDeeplinks` into `<MarketSizeTile />` on `/dashboard/market-size` — **shipped 2026-07-25**. Closes the natural next-tick after P3-ibisworld-deeplinks: the pure lib (`web/src/lib/market/ibisworld-deeplinks.ts`) shipped standalone with no founder-facing surface consuming it, so a founder browsing `/dashboard/market-size` saw the ABS TAM/SAM/SOM anchor but no way to jump to the underlying IBISWorld report. This tick adds a Chapter-3-aligned "Cite an IBISWorld report" block at the bottom of the tile that lists up to 3 freshest IBISWorld deep-links for the founder's ANZSIC class (falling back to a keyword search when no ANZSIC anchor is available) plus a "Browse the AU industry index ↗" link to the top-level portal for founders whose sector isn't in the seeded set. Zero schema churn, zero new API route, zero new client fetch — the pure module is a fixture projection so the RSC pipeline stays server-only. Four surfaces touched: (1) `web/src/components/dashboard/market-size-tile.helpers.ts` — new pure `sortIbisworldDeeplinksForTile(matches, cap)` helper. Sorts by `publishedYear` desc (freshest first), tie-broken by `anzsicCode` alphabetical for deterministic re-render order. `cap` clamps to non-negative integer via `Math.max(0, Math.floor(cap))` with a `Number.isFinite` guard so `NaN` / negative / non-integer inputs collapse to `[]` instead of erroring. Never mutates the input array (spreads via `[...matches]` before sort). (2) `web/src/components/dashboard/market-size-tile.tsx` — new optional `ibisworld?: IbisworldSearchResult | null` prop threaded into a new inline `<IbisworldDeeplinksBlock />` sub-component. Constant `IBISWORLD_TILE_CAP = 3` pins the display cap at file top so the tile doesn't outgrow its visual budget. The block renders `data-testid="market-size-ibisworld"` + `data-report-count={count}` on the wrapper, `data-testid="market-size-ibisworld-row"` + `data-anzsic={code}` + `data-ibisworld-report-id={id}` on each row so a future Playwright spec can attach without helper edits. Zero-report empty state ("No IBISWorld report is seeded for this sector yet — use the AU industry index above to search directly") mirrors the tile's existing empty-state posture. `IBISWORLD_DEEPLINKS_DISCLAIMER` surfaced verbatim inside the block via `ibisworld.disclaimer` so the s766B / paywalled-resource hedge travels with the deep-link surface even if a downstream renderer forgets to attach it. (3) `web/src/app/dashboard/market-size/page.tsx` — server-side call to `findIbisworldDeeplinks({ anzsicCode: result?.industry.anzsicCode ?? explicitAnzsic, sector: !ibisworldAnzsic && keyword ? keyword : undefined, limit: 5 })`. Precedence: ABS-resolved ANZSIC → explicit `?anzsic=` param → keyword fallback. Skips the lookup entirely when neither ANZSIC nor keyword is available (tile stays empty rather than firing an "all-sectors" firehose). (4) `web/src/components/dashboard/market-size-tile.helpers.test.ts` — new `sortIbisworldDeeplinksForTile` describe block with 5 vitest cases pinning (a) empty input regardless of cap, (b) cap zero / negative / NaN → empty, (c) publishedYear desc + slice cap, (d) equal-year alphabetical anzsicCode tie-break, (e) input-mutation guard. Full 62 tests green across `src/lib/market` + `src/components/dashboard/market-size-tile.helpers.test.ts` (57 existing + 5 new). Typecheck `./node_modules/.bin/tsc --noEmit --project tsconfig.json` green (exit 0). Closes the founder-facing consumer half of the P3-ibisworld-deeplinks tick — the lib was shipped in isolation waiting for a surface to render it; `/dashboard/market-size` now surfaces the deep-links alongside the ABS anchor so Chapter 3 (Market Research) has a single tile that answers "how big is my market" + "where's the IBISWorld report I can cite". Playwright coverage of the new block (asserting `market-size-ibisworld` presence, exactly one of empty-state vs report list, `data-ibisworld-report-id` on populated rows, disclaimer verbatim under `/not personal financial product advice/i` regex) shipped 2026-07-25 as P3-ibisworld-tile-e2e (see below).

### Round 8 follow-ups (spun off from P0 phase-gap matrix — §1 phase 12)

- **P9-esic-round-marketing-gate-ui** founder-facing explicit "Marketed as ESIC-qualifying" checkbox on the fundraise-round wizard (§P9-esic-round-marketing-gate follow-up "founder-facing checkbox on the fundraise-round wizard that sets `marketedAsEsic: true` explicitly rather than relying on heuristic scans") — **shipped 2026-07-25**. Closes the last outstanding leg of the P9-esic-round-marketing-gate chain. The server-side auto-firing gate (P9-esic-round-marketing-gate) closes the s1041H exposure regardless of UI wiring — even a founder who never opens the wizard's marketing block still trips the gate if the round name / pitch text / tags carry ESIC-flavoured terms. This tick lets the founder *pre-declare* the intent so the gate fires deterministically (no reliance on keyword matches) and the fundraise UI carries the AFSL / s1041H hedge before the POST hits the server. Zero server-side churn — the `/api/fundraise` route already accepts `marketedAsEsic?: boolean` on the POST body (`web/src/app/api/fundraise/route.ts:25` + line 113) and `detectEsicMarketing()` already fires on the explicit flag (P9-esic-round-marketing-gate). Three client-side edits + one helper extension: (a) `web/src/app/workspace/fundraise/fundraise-gate.helpers.ts` — `FundraisePostBodyInput` + `FundraisePostBodyWire` grow an optional `marketedAsEsic?: boolean` slot; `buildFundraisePostBody()` only emits `marketedAsEsic: true` on the wire when the flag is true so an unticked checkbox never over-declares intent (the route defaults `undefined → false`), keeping the wire body as minimal as it was before this tick when the founder leaves the box unticked. (b) `web/src/app/workspace/fundraise/fundraise-gate.helpers.test.ts` — grows a 4-case block covering the emit-when-true, omit-when-unset, omit-when-explicitly-false, and cross-instrument-type × wholesale-independence branches: `expect(wire.marketedAsEsic).toBe(true)` when set, `expect("marketedAsEsic" in wire).toBe(false)` when unset / explicit false, and `retailMarketed.wholesaleOnly === false && retailMarketed.marketedAsEsic === true` on the retail-marketed-ESIC path (the key s1041H exposure the checkbox exists to declare). All 28 vitest cases in the file green (24 existing + 4 new). (c) `web/src/app/workspace/fundraise/fundraise-client.tsx` — new `marketedAsEsic` React state (`useState<boolean>(false)` conservative default so a founder isn't opted-in to blocking mode without a click), threaded through the `buildFundraisePostBody()` call in `calculateDilution()`, and rendered as an amber-highlighted opt-in card immediately beneath the P6b `Wholesale-only round` card in Step 0. BlockID will refuse to save the round (HTTP 412) unless a fresh eligible ESIC self-assessment is on file, because misleading conduct under s1041H Corporations Act 2001 (Cth) doesn't turn on the wholesale/retail distinction." `data-testid="fundraise-marketed-as-esic"` on the checkbox so a future Playwright spec can attach without helper edits. - **P9-esic-round-marketing-gate** auto-firing ESIC eligibility gate on rounds that pitch the Div 360 offset (§1 phase 9 P0 gap follow-up on P9-esic-self-assessment) — **shipped 2026-07-25**. Closes the last outstanding §1 phase-9 P0 gap. Previously `/api/fundraise` only 412-blocked on `body.wholesaleOnly=true`, which conflated the investor-eligibility category (s708(8)/(11) sophisticated) with the *marketing* signal (pitch materials claim ESIC 20% offset). A retail round that markets ESIC still fires s1041H misleading-conduct exposure if no fresh eligible assessment is on file — the previous gate left that path open. New pure detector at `web/src/lib/compliance/esic-marketing-gate.ts` — `detectEsicMarketing(input) → {marketed, signals, disclaimer}` reads 5 marketing-metadata slots off the fundraise POST body (`marketedAsEsic` explicit flag, `pitchClaimsEsic` explicit alt, `roundName` free-text scan, `pitchDescription` free-text scan, `tags[]` string scan) and returns a machine-readable signal list drawn from a 7-way enum (`explicit_marketed_flag` / `explicit_pitch_flag` / `round_name_mentions_esic` / `pitch_description_mentions_esic` / `round_name_mentions_offset` / `pitch_description_mentions_offset` / `tag_mentions_esic`). Text scanners are case-insensitive with word-boundary anchoring — `ESIC_TOKEN_RE` matches `esic`, `early-stage innovation company`, or `div 360` / `division 360`; `ESIC_OFFSET_RE` matches `20% offset`, `20-per-cent offset`, `10-year CGT`, or `CGT exemption`. Non-string tag entries are silently ignored so a mistyped client cannot throw. `ESIC_MARKETING_DISCLAIMER` cites Corps Act 2001 (Cth) s1041H (misleading or deceptive conduct in relation to a financial product) + s923B (AFSL/TPB boundary — BlockID does NOT certify ESIC status). Wired into `web/src/app/api/fundraise/route.ts` — when `marketing.marketed` fires, `requireEligible=true` on `assertESICEligibleOrWarn` regardless of the `wholesaleOnly` category, so the retail-marketed-ESIC path now 412-blocks. Analytics action tag flips to `fundraise_round_create_marketed_esic` so ops dashboards can slice the marketed-vs-non-marketed block rate. Both the 412 (blocked) response body and the 200 (allowed) response body surface `marketing_signals[]` + `marketing_disclaimer` alongside the existing gate envelope so the founder-facing UI can render the specific signal that fired ("your round name contains 'ESIC'") plus the s1041H / s923B disclaimer without a second round-trip. 14 vitest cases in `web/src/lib/compliance/esic-marketing-gate.test.ts` covering the empty-input baseline, both explicit flags, the false-does-not-fire guard, round-name + pitch-description ESIC / offset / CGT scans, unrelated-Series-A negative case, tag matching, multi-signal collection, null / undefined defensive path, non-string tag defensive path, and disclaimer round-trip with s1041H + s923B anchor grep. All 199 tests in `src/lib/compliance/` green (185 existing + 14 new); typecheck `npx tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json` exit 0. Founder-facing explicit checkbox on the fundraise-round wizard that sets `marketedAsEsic: true` (rather than relying on heuristic scans) remains a natural follow-up under P9-esic-round-marketing-gate-ui once the sibling fundraise-wizard agent unlocks — this tick ships the server-side auto-firing gate so the s1041H exposure closes today regardless of UI wiring.
- **P12d-redomicile-decision** author Chapter 12 Scheme-of-Arrangement / redomicile decision-tree section (§1 phase 12 P2 gap "Scheme-of-Arrangement / redomicile decision-tree doc (advanced but relevant for successful exits)") — **shipped 2026-07-25**. Closes the last outstanding §1 phase-12 content gap. Chapter 12 (`12-exit`) now carries a `sections[]` entry `redomicile-decision-tree` (heading + 8-bullet body EN + VI) via the previously-declared `Chapter.sections?: ChapterSection[]` slot (`web/src/lib/guide/startup-journey.ts:67`). The section walks the redomicile decision end-to-end: (a) Atlassian's 2022-10-03 UK Plc → Delaware redomicile via a UK Companies Act 2006 Part 26 Scheme of Arrangement (sanctioned by the UK High Court — TIMELINE lines 175-181), (b) the three canonical AU-founder scenarios (Delaware acquirer needing Section 368(a) reorganisation treatment, US-IPO / dual-class per P1m, US-resident cap-table concentration creating PFIC exposure), (c) the three AU mechanisms (Corps Act 2001 (Cth) Part 5.1 s411 member Scheme of Arrangement with ASIC review + Federal Court sanction + 75% class-by-value / 50% by-number majority; s657A/s658A takeover-bid restructure; direct asset transfer + winding-up — the wrong answer for an operating co), (d) AU tax on the seller side (CGT event A1 ITAA 1997 s104-10 on exchange unless Subdiv 124-M scrip-for-scrip rollover applies — 80% voting-share test + same-class-offer test + all-shares-consideration test), (e) FIRB pre-lodgment under Foreign Acquisitions and Takeovers Act 1975 (Cth) with 2026 A$339M non-sensitive threshold + sensitive-sector list + foreign-govt-influenced A$0 trigger + 30-60 days runway, (f) the US-side cost founders rarely model (CFC status under IRC §957 for the surviving AU op-co, IRC §1441 withholding on AU-resident dividends reducible under Article 10 of the Australia–US Convention, GILTI under IRC §951A, PFIC re-emergence risk under IRC §1297), (g) timeline + cost (4-9 months, A$300k-A$800k for a straightforward scheme; add A$150k-A$300k for a US S-4 registration if concurrently listing; A$200k-A$400k extra for a UK Plc intermediary step), (h) the BlockID.au boundary — CFO exit-readiness (`/dashboard/exit-readiness` — see P12b-tile) surfaces a 'redomicile scenario' toggle on the trigger scenarios, au-compliance drafts the FIRB pre-lodgment questionnaire, CLO scans change-of-control clauses 90 days out, but BlockID does NOT auto-draft the scheme booklet, Delaware certificate, Subdiv 124-M private ruling, or FIRB Form 202 (s911A + s923B Corps Act 2001 (Cth) financial-services / legal-services boundary guard). Both marketing (`/guide/12-exit`) and workspace (`/workspace/guide/12-exit`) surfaces render the new section via the existing `c.sections?.map()` blocks (no page edits needed — reuses the P1j / P1l / P1m / P11-acquisition-pattern wiring). - **P12d-redomicile-wizard** interactive redomicile decision wizard mounted on `/dashboard/exit-readiness` — **shipped 2026-07-25**. Closes the P12d-redomicile-decision follow-up ("an interactive 'run my redomicile through the decision tree' wizard on `/dashboard/exit-readiness`"). Follows the P11-acquisition-wizard-ui pattern — pure lib + form helpers + client component + one-line mount + colocated vitest, no schema churn, no new API routes, no persistence. Four new files + one two-line page edit: (a) `web/src/lib/exits/redomicile-decision-check.ts` — pure `assessRedomicile(input) → RedomicileDecision` returning `{recommendation, triggers[], mechanism, pfic_exposure_flagged, subdiv_124m_rollover_available, can_fund_redomicile, warnings[], next_steps[], disclaimer}`. Recommendation matrix: pre-Series-A → `hold` (overrides every trigger), zero triggers → `hold`, single trigger → `prepare` (book a call), 2+ triggers + burn ≥ A$100k floor → `proceed` (engage the M&A partner), 2+ triggers + burn < A$100k → `reconsider` (raise a bridge or defer). Triggers: `delaware_acquirer` / `us_listing_dual_class` (requires BOTH `plans_us_listing` AND `wants_dual_class_founder_control` so a US-listing plan without dual-class doesn't over-fire) / `pfic_exposure` (US-resident cap-table ≥ 50%) / `ip_already_in_delaware` / `scrip_only_offer`. Mechanism resolves to `scheme_of_arrangement_s411` (Corps Act 2001 (Cth) Part 5.1 s411 member scheme, Federal Court sanction, 75% class-by-value / 50% by-number) for the Delaware acquirer / scrip-only / dual-class paths; `takeover_bid` (s657A / s658A) for the PFIC-only path; `direct_asset_transfer` reserved for the operating-co-with-no-AU-side-ops edge case. `pfic_exposure_flagged=true` when US-resident cap-table ≥ `PFIC_CAP_TABLE_THRESHOLD_PCT=0.5` (IRC §1297 exposure); `subdiv_124m_rollover_available=true` when the offer is scrip-only (ITAA 1997 Subdiv 124-M eligibility signal); `can_fund_redomicile=true` when annual burn ≥ `REDOMICILE_FLOOR_BURN_AUD=100_000` against the `REDOMICILE_MIN_BUDGET_AUD=300_000` / `REDOMICILE_MAX_BUDGET_AUD=800_000` scheme-cost band. Fixed `REDOMICILE_DISCLAIMER` cites UK Companies Act 2006 Part 26, Corps Act 2001 (Cth) Part 5.1 s411, ITAA 1997 Subdiv 124-M, Foreign Acquisitions and Takeovers Act 1975 (Cth), US IRC §957 CFC / §1297 PFIC / §951A GILTI, and Corps Act s911A + s923B financial-services / legal-services boundary — so no render surface can leak a recommendation without the "engage a specialist cross-border tax + corporate lawyer" hedge (BlockID.au does NOT auto-draft the scheme booklet, Delaware certificate, Subdiv 124-M private ruling, or FIRB Form 202). Defensive input handling: non-finite / negative pfic pct → null (no false-positive PFIC flag); non-finite / negative burn → null (falls into `can_fund_redomicile=false`); pfic pct > 100 clamps to 100 without erroring. (b) `web/src/lib/exits/redomicile-decision-check.test.ts` — 16 vitest cases across 4 describe blocks: recommendation branches (holds on empty + pre-Series-A + single-trigger prepare + 2+ triggers proceed + 2+ triggers reconsider when burn < floor), trigger detection (PFIC threshold boundary, 0-1 vs 0-100 pct normalisation, us_listing_dual_class requires both flags, scrip-only ⇒ subdiv_124m_rollover_available + trigger, cash+shares warning fires when scrip-only=false but Delaware acquirer, ASX Listing Rule 6.9 warning when dual-class without acquirer), defensive input (NaN / negative tolerance + pfic pct > 100 clamp), disclaimer + constants (disclaimer attached on every branch + statutory anchor grep for Scheme of Arrangement / s411 / Subdiv 124-M / FIRB / IRC §957 / §1297 / §951A / s911A / s923B + budget/floor constants pinned). (c) `web/src/components/dashboard/redomicile-wizard.helpers.ts` — form-state helpers `RedomicileWizardFormState` (8 fields — 6 booleans + 2 string-shaped numbers so `<input type="number">` values round-trip cleanly) + `makeEmptyRedomicileWizardFormState()` (conservative-AU-founder baseline: every toggle off, both numbers blank, so the wizard opens on `hold` rather than pre-selling redomicile) + `toRedomicileInput(state)` (blank numeric fields collapse to null so the pure lib's "unspecified" branches fire correctly; non-finite input coerces to null) + `pickRedomicileBand(recommendation, state) → "green"|"amber"|"red"|"grey"` — grey when the form is entirely blank (invites input), green for `hold` (nothing to do is a healthy outcome), amber for `prepare` / `proceed` (deliberately NOT green even when triggered — the honest posture is "engage a specialist lawyer first"), red for `reconsider` (triggers without runway). `REDOMICILE_HEADLINE` copy pack (4 distinct headlines) + `REDOMICILE_MECHANISM_LABEL` (statutory anchor per mechanism — s411 / s657A / operating-co warning). (d) `web/src/components/dashboard/redomicile-wizard.helpers.test.ts` — 12 vitest cases across 4 describe blocks: `makeEmptyRedomicileWizardFormState` conservative-baseline defaults; `toRedomicileInput` (blank-collapse-to-null + populated round-trip + non-finite drop + boolean passthrough); `pickRedomicileBand` all 5 branches anchored on real `assessRedomicile()` calls (grey on blank; green on `hold` with founder-typed pre-Series-A; amber on `prepare` single-trigger; amber on `proceed` 2+ triggers funded; red on `reconsider` 2+ triggers unfunded); copy packs (`REDOMICILE_HEADLINE` distinct-per-recommendation + non-empty + `REDOMICILE_MECHANISM_LABEL` statutory anchor grep). (e) `web/src/components/dashboard/redomicile-wizard-client.tsx` — `"use client"` component with two sections (Triggers — 6 toggles: delaware acquirer / us listing / dual-class founder control / scrip-only offer / IP in Delaware / pre-Series-A; Cap table & runway — 2 number inputs: us-resident cap-table pct + annual burn AUD) and a live result banner keyed on `pickRedomicileBand(recommendation, state)` with distinct BAND_STYLES per band (green = emerald / CheckCircle2, amber = amber / AlertTriangle, red = red / XCircle, grey = surface / Info) surfacing (i) `REDOMICILE_HEADLINE[recommendation]` headline + Icon, (ii) triggers-detected count + resolved mechanism label with statutory anchor inline, (iii) warnings[] list with ShieldAlert icon (fires per-branch — pre-Series-A override, PFIC exposure narrative, cash+shares vs scrip-only mismatch, ASX Listing Rule 6.9 dual-class dead-end, runway-shortfall vs A$300-800k scheme cost), (iv) next_steps[] list with Info icon (per-recommendation copy — "do nothing" for hold, "book a preliminary call" for prepare, "engage the M&A lead partner + budget A$300-800k + appoint an Independent Expert + model Subdiv 83A-C ESS tax hit" for proceed, "do NOT sign a redomicile mandate today — raise a bridge or defer" for reconsider, plus a fixed folder-1+2 data-room reminder that always fires), (v) fixed `REDOMICILE_DISCLAIMER` footer. `data-testid="redomicile-wizard"` + `data-band` + `data-recommendation` on the section root; `data-testid` on every input + toggle + result / warnings / next-steps blocks so a future Playwright spec can attach without helper edits. (f) `web/src/app/dashboard/exit-readiness/page.tsx` — one-line import + one-line mount of `<RedomicileWizardClient />` immediately beneath `<AcquisitionWizardClient />` in the founder shell. Rowe 2014 secondary, OpsGenie 2018, Trello 2017) are backed by fixture rows; (4) `getAuComparableExits` — sector filter case-insensitively (`"FinTech"` → 100% `fintech`); buyerType filter; minYear + limit + newest-first sort; unknown sector → `[]`; (5) `summariseAuExits` — empty-input zero-shape; full-fixture medians + buyerType-count + latestYear ≥ 2023; odd + even median branches both exercised. - **P5-cohort** cohort retention chart auto-draw component (Folder 5 phase-5 P1 gap) — **shipped 2026-07-24**. Closes §1 phase 5 P1 gap "cohort chart auto-draw component (guide claims it; no `web/src/lib/traction/cohort-chart.ts` exists)" — the Chapter 5 (PMF / Early Traction) body promises "Retention-cohort chart — auto-drawn from the weekly buckets into the DataRoom under traction/" at `web/src/lib/guide/startup-journey.ts:364` but no module existed to compute or render the matrix. New pure module `web/src/lib/traction/cohort-chart.ts` — `computeWeeklyCohortRetention(signups, activities, {reference_date?, cohort_count?, week_count?}) → CohortMatrix` and `renderCohortRetentionSvg(matrix, opts?) → string` mirroring the reseller/cohort-velocity.ts + startup-growth-phases.ts posture (dependency-free, string in / string out) so both the maths and the SVG can regression-test without touching Supabase, React, or a browser DOM. Cohort key = Monday 00:00 UTC of the signup week (ISO 8601 aligned; JS `getUTCDay()` 0-Sunday shifted to 7 before subtracting so Sunday signups land in the prior Monday's cohort not a phantom Sunday-anchored week). First-signup-wins per user_id so a re-registration cannot silently shift a user into a later cohort. Retention decimals in `[0,1]` — the SVG formats them as `Math.round(val * 100)%` so cell text stays legible at report scale. `null` (not `0`) surfaces for weeks that have not yet elapsed since the cohort start, so a young cohort renders as a truncated row rather than misleading false churn (Mixpanel + Amplitude follow the same rule; investors read that convention). Empty cohort weeks drop entirely so gaps in signup activity do not shift row alignment. Row order = oldest-first (top → bottom) so W0 lands top-left, matching the analyst-facing grid convention. `meets_min_buckets` boolean flips at `MIN_COHORT_BUCKETS = 4` — the "signal is investor-defensible" floor (Chapter 5 CTA says "at least two consecutive weeks" but 4 is the PMF-signal minimum investors expect, per Sean Ellis + Rahul Vohra's PMF-survey conventions). Defaults: `DEFAULT_COHORT_COUNT = 8` (trailing 8 weeks of cohorts) + `DEFAULT_WEEK_COUNT = 8` (8 retention columns) so a founder with 200 weeks of data cannot blow up a report PDF. `cohortCellColour(value)` maps decimals to a 5-band heatmap (`null → slate-200`, `0 → slate-100`, `<0.2 → red-200`, `<0.4 → orange-200`, `<0.6 → amber-200`, `<0.8 → green-200`, `≥0.8 → green-300`) that reads in both light + dark PDF backgrounds. SVG renderer surfaces an empty-state hint when no cohorts exist ("No cohort data yet — log at least 4 weeks of signups") and a below-min-signal amber-text warning when `meets_min_buckets = false`, so the founder sees the investor-readiness gap on the same visual instead of shipping a green-looking chart with 2 rows. Test coverage `web/src/lib/traction/cohort-chart.test.ts` — 13 vitest cases across 3 describe blocks: (a) `computeWeeklyCohortRetention` (8 cases pinning Monday-anchored cohort bucketing + oldest-first row order + empty-cohort drop, first-signup-wins-per-user_id, retention arithmetic across a 4-user × 4-week fixture (0.75/0.25/0.5/0.25 pattern), null-for-not-yet-elapsed weeks on this-week cohorts, trailing `cohort_count` window drops old signups, malformed input tolerance (invalid ISO + empty user_id both silently skipped), `meets_min_buckets` flip at 4 consecutive cohorts, stable-defaults pin), (b) `cohortCellColour` (1 case pinning all 8 colour-band branches incl. `null` + `NaN` + `0` edge cases + verbatim hex codes so a downstream renderer cannot silently drift), (c) `renderCohortRetentionSvg` (4 cases pinning `<svg xmlns=...>` prefix + one `<rect>` per cell + integer-% cell labels + cohort_week + `n={size}` chip render, empty-state hint text present + `MIN_COHORT_BUCKETS` count grep, below-min-signal hint fires when cohort_count < 4 and *doesn't* fire when count ≥ 4 (regression guard), and cell/padding options scale the SVG `width` attribute correctly). All 13 tests green. Typecheck `npx tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json` green (leaf lib with no upstream imports beyond `vitest` in the test file, so a broken helper cannot cascade). Downstream wiring (Chapter 5 CTA renders the SVG into `/dashboard/svi` or the SVI report PDF's Traction section; nightly cron writes `cohort-retention.pdf` into `dataroom_files` under folder 5 traction/) remains a follow-up under P5-cohort-wire — this tick ships the pure fixture + helpers only so any surface that needs a cohort retention heatmap can import it without waiting.

- **P3b** `/api/abs/lookup` route wrapper (Folder 5 phase-3 P1 gap follow-up on P3a) — **shipped 2026-07-24**. Public GET route at `web/src/app/api/abs/lookup/route.ts` mirroring the `/api/abr/lookup` + `/api/asic/extract` posture (no auth, `dynamic="force-dynamic"`, `runtime="nodejs"`, 1 h edge cache on 200s). Query surface: `?anzsic=<ANZSIC 2006 class code>` OR `?keyword=<industry term>` (`?sector=` accepted as an alias so the Chapter 3 CTA can use founder-vernacular field names without a client-side rename) plus optional `?addressablePct=0..1` (default 0.2) and `?targetSharePct=0..1` (default 0.01). Delegates to `estimateTamSamSom({anzsicCode, keyword, addressablePct, targetSharePct})` from `web/src/lib/market/au-market-lookup.ts` — no logic duplicated; pct parser clamps to `[0,1]` and falls back to defaults on non-finite input (matches the `parseFloat`-with-clamp posture the ABN route uses on the modulus check). 200 body surface: `{industry: {anzsic_code, division, label, keywords, cagr, business_count, sources, notes}, tam_aud, sam_aud, som_aud, addressable_pct, target_share_pct, suggestions, disclaimer}` — `suggestions[]` (up to 4 sibling ANZSIC classes for the keyword the founder actually typed) is populated on keyword lookups so a founder who typed "software" gets a pivot list back to IaaS / marketplace / other neighbouring classes; ANZSIC-pinned callers get an empty `suggestions[]` because they already picked the class. Fixed `AU_MARKET_LOOKUP_DISCLAIMER` (ABS-provenance + "refine with primary research before quoting to investors" hedge, cites s766B Corps Act 2001 (Cth) at source) travels on every 200 so downstream renderers cannot leak an anchor without the AFSL guard. Error posture: `400` `missing_lookup_input` when neither anzsic nor keyword supplied; `404` `industry_not_found` when the input matches no seeded row (with the raw input echoed back in `input.{anzsic,keyword}` so a keyword-typo caller can see what they sent). Test coverage `web/src/app/api/abs/lookup/route.test.ts` — 7 vitest cases pinning (a) 400 branch + machine-readable error code, (b) known ANZSIC returns TAM/SAM/SOM at default 20%/1% + disclaimer round-trip + 1h cache-control + empty suggestions[], (c) keyword lookup returns top pick + suggestions[] excludes the pick + all sibling entries have distinct anzsic_code, (d) `?sector=` alias resolves the same as `?keyword=`, (e) addressablePct/targetSharePct honour explicit values + clamp overshoot (5 → 1, -3 → 0) + fall back to defaults on garbage/empty input, (f) unknown ANZSIC → 404 + `industry_not_found` + input echo, (g) unmatched keyword → 404 + `industry_not_found` + keyword echo. All 7 tests green. Typecheck `npx tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json` green. Closes the P3a "Downstream wiring (Chapter 3 CTA renders TAM/SAM/SOM anchor into `/dashboard/svi` or the CMO deep-pass; `/api/abs/lookup` route wrapper if we later want a JSON endpoint mirroring the abn.ts + acn.ts route pattern) deferred to P3b" note — the route half now ships. Chapter 3 CTA + CMO deep-pass consumer wiring (which surface calls this route with the founder's project.industry) remains a follow-up under P3c because it needs the sibling market-research agent to pick which panel renders the response — this tick ships the endpoint contract so the surface can consume it without waiting.

- **P3a** AU industry-size lookup module (Folder 5 phase-3 P1 gap "IBISWorld / ABS lookup not surfaced; no `/api/abs/lookup` stub") — **shipped 2026-07-24**. New pure module `web/src/lib/market/au-market-lookup.ts` — offline seeded lookup keyed by ANZSIC 2006 class code (Australian and New Zealand Standard Industrial Classification, jointly published by ABS + Stats NZ). 12 industry snapshots covering the divisions AU startups actually operate in — SaaS (J5810), Data-processing / IaaS (J5921), Fintech (K6419), E-commerce (G4720), Healthtech (Q8511), Deeptech / R&D (M6910), Edtech (P8102), Agritech (A0169), Cleantech (D2611), Legaltech (M6913), HR-tech (M6931), Marketplaces (N7292) — every row anchored on ≥1 ABS release (8155.0 Australian Industry, 8501.0 Retail Trade, 8104.0 R&D-Businesses, 4660.0 Energy Account, 7503.0 Value-of-Ag-Commodities) plus a public IBISWorld / AusIndustry / RBA URL. Public API surface: `lookupByAnzsic(code)` (case + whitespace tolerant, null-safe), `searchByKeyword(query, limit=5)` (scored — exact-keyword-hit +100, label-prefix +40, label-substring +20, keyword-substring +10; ties broken by larger TAM), `listCoveredDivisions()` (division-key-sorted rollup for the tooling introspection layer), and `estimateTamSamSom({anzsicCode?, keyword?, addressablePct=0.2, targetSharePct=0.01})` returning `{industry, tamAud, samAud, somAud, addressablePct, targetSharePct, disclaimer}`. Fixed disclaimer `AU_MARKET_LOOKUP_DISCLAIMER` attached to every `estimateTamSamSom` result and exported for other consumers (e.g. `web/src/lib/agents/cfo-tam-sam-som.ts`) so no rendering surface can leak an anchor figure without the ABS-provenance hedge. Design rationale in the module JSDoc: offline seed keeps Chapter 3 available when ABS SDMX is under maintenance; live probes are already prioritised on the P0 raise-blocker path (ABR / ASIC / GST) so this stays anchor-only. 19 vitest cases in `web/src/lib/market/au-market-lookup.test.ts` across 5 describe blocks — (a) seed integrity (unique 5-char ANZSIC codes matching `^[A-Z]\d{4}$`, ≥1 ABS/AusIndustry/RBA source per row with https URL + publishedYear ≥ 2020, sane TAM/CAGR/businessCount ranges, every division key resolves to `ANZSIC_DIVISIONS`), (b) `lookupByAnzsic` (known-code match, case + whitespace tolerance, null for empty/unknown/null/undefined), (c) `searchByKeyword` (exact SaaS hit → J5810, fintech → K-division preference, blank / gibberish returns empty, limit respected), (d) `listCoveredDivisions` (counts sum to seed size, alphabetically sorted by division key), (e) `estimateTamSamSom` (SAM = TAM × addressablePct + SOM = SAM × sharePct arithmetic, default 20% + 1% percentages, out-of-range clamp to [0,1], keyword-fallback branch, null-on-no-match branch, `AU_MARKET_LOOKUP_DISCLAIMER` attached branch). All 19 tests green. Typecheck N/A (leaf lib with no upstream imports beyond `vitest` in the test file). Closes §1 phase 3 P1 gap "IBISWorld/ABS lookup". Downstream wiring (Chapter 3 CTA renders TAM/SAM/SOM anchor into `/dashboard/svi` or the CMO deep-pass; `/api/abs/lookup` route wrapper if we later want a JSON endpoint mirroring the abn.ts + acn.ts route pattern) deferred to P3b — this tick ships the pure fixture + helpers only so any surface that needs an AU industry anchor can import it without waiting.

- **P10-s708counter** s708(1) small-scale personal-offer counter (Folder 2/10 phase-10 P1 gap "s708(1) counter — running total of investors/$ in trailing 12 mo") — **shipped 2026-07-24**. New pure module `web/src/lib/compliance/s708-small-scale-counter.ts` exports `assessS708SmallScale({events, now?, preview?}) → S708CounterResult` mirroring the P1i `gst-threshold.ts` + P1n `wgea-threshold.ts` result-envelope shape so downstream cron / dashboard-panel wiring can reuse the same `{status, reason, next_action, disclaimer}` contract. Anchors on Corporations Act 2001 (Cth) s708(1)(a) (20-investor cap), s708(1)(b) (A$2,000,000 dollar cap), s708(3) (rolling 12-month window), and s707 on-sale rule for secondary transfers within 12 months of original issue (both `primary` and `secondary_on_sale` `offer_type` values feed the same counter — per s707(3) they consume the same headroom). 3-state classifier (`ok` / `warn` / `block`): warn amber-band when investor headroom ≤ 1 OR dollar headroom ≤ A$200,000, block red-band when either cap has already been reached OR when the optional `preview: {new_investors, amount_aud}` projection would breach either cap. Fixed `S708_SMALL_SCALE_DISCLAIMER` names the related-body-corporate / spouse / s707 on-sale caveats and cites Australian securities counsel — no rendering surface can leak the counter without the hedge (avoids s923B / s911A Corps Act exposure). Result envelope also carries `window_start_iso` / `window_end_iso` (trailing-365-day span computed from `now`), `investors_remaining` / `dollars_remaining_aud` (clamped ≥ 0), and `would_breach_investor_cap` / `would_breach_dollar_cap` (only set when preview supplied). Input tolerant of malformed events: bad ISO dates, blank/whitespace-only investor_ids, non-finite amounts, and negative amounts are silently dropped rather than thrown so callers can feed a `share_register` row set without pre-filtering. Investor de-duplication via `Set<investor_id>` (same email subscribing across tranches counts once, matching the ASIC "issue" counting convention). `next_action` copy is status-differentiated: green surfaces the `/dashboard/data-room` Folder 1 item 1.6 (Register of Members) logging nudge, amber pushes founders to run their next tranche through the `preview` mode before accepting, red enumerates the s708(8)/(10)/(11) wholesale + s738G CSF + full disclosure document escape hatches AND the s708(4) issue-to-existing-holders carve-out. Tests: 12 vitest cases in `web/src/lib/compliance/s708-small-scale-counter.test.ts` across 3 describe blocks — (1) window boundary (trailing-365d drop, de-dup within window, malformed-event silent skip), (2) status classification (ok/warn-investor/warn-dollar/block-investor/block-dollar branches at the exact statutory + amber-band edges), (3) preview mode (both cap-breach branches + a pass-through). All 12 tests green (`npx vitest run src/lib/compliance/s708-small-scale-counter.test.ts` — 1 file, 12 passed). Typecheck `npx tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json` green (exit 0). Closes §1 phase 10 P1 gap "s708(1) counter (running total of investors/$ in trailing 12 mo)"; downstream wiring (Chapter 10 CTA `/dashboard/fundraise` running-counter tile + fundraise-route pre-flight preview gate + `dataroom_files` register-of-members read adapter) deferred to P10-s708counter-wire because it needs a sibling UI + share-register-schema pass. Pure primitive shipped standalone so the sibling agent has a stable contract to build against.

Anchors on Corporations Act 2001 (Cth) s707 (on-sale) + Blackbird / AirTree / Square Peg standard SHA pre-emption templates (15-business-day notice window default via `ROFR_DEFAULT_NOTICE_DAYS = 15`, tag-along trigger at `TAG_ALONG_FOUNDER_BLOCK_PCT = 0.5` when a founder sells ≥ half their block to a non-related third party, s707 on-sale window at 365 days). 7-state classifier (`not_applicable | pending_notice | notice_period_open | notice_period_closed | fully_waived | partially_exercised | fully_exercised`): pro-rata entitlement across non-seller pre-emption holders using each holder's share-of-eligible-pool (rounded to whole shares), waivers de-dup via `Set<holder_id>`, exercises capped at pro-rata slice with over-subscription warned (not silently swallowed — over-subscription rights are a separate SHA mechanic the calculator stubs), silence past the deadline surfaces as `notice_period_closed` with "deemed waiver" next-action copy so a founder can move to lodgment. Fixed `ROFR_DISCLAIMER` names the constitution + SHA precedence + the drag-along / tag-along / buyer-substitution stubs so no rendering surface can leak a classification without the hedge (avoids s911A / s923B / s1041H Corps Act exposure). All 21 tests green (`npx vitest run src/lib/compliance/rofr-workflow.test.ts` — 1 file, 21 passed). Typecheck `npx tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json` green (exit 0). - **P1n-calendar-events** fold WGEA + Modern Slavery detector output into the compliance calendar (.ics) — **shipped 2026-07-24**.   - `web/src/lib/compliance/calendar.ts` — `ComplianceEventKind` grows two members `wgea_report` + `modern_slavery_statement`; `BuildComplianceCalendarOptions` grows two nullable slots `wgea?: WGEAResult | null` + `modernSlavery?: ModernSlaveryResult | null`. Two new pure emitters: (a) `buildWgeaEvents(wgea, now, horizonMonths)` — returns a single `wgea_report` event on `wgea.next_report_due_iso` when `is_relevant_employer=true` AND the deadline is inside the 12-month horizon; UID `wgea-report-{yyyy}@blockid.au` deterministic + stable across re-subscribes; summary "WGEA public report {yyyy} — lodge with agency"; description cites WGE Act 2012 (Cth) s3 + the 1-Apr → 31-Mar reporting period + the six gender-equality-indicator dataset requirement + the WGEA reporting URL; 14-day `VALARM` matches BAS / ASIC / R&D. (b) `buildModernSlaveryEvents(ms, now, horizonMonths)` — returns a single `modern_slavery_statement` event on `ms.statement_due_iso` when `is_reporting_entity=true` AND the deadline is inside the horizon; UID `modern-slavery-statement-{yyyy}@blockid.au` deterministic; summary "Modern Slavery statement {yyyy} — lodge with register"; description cites Modern Slavery Act 2018 (Cth) s5 (reporting-entity definition) + s13(2) (principal-governing-body approval + responsible-member signature) + s16 (seven mandatory reporting criteria) + the Attorney-General's register URL; 14-day `VALARM`. Both emitters follow the same short-circuit pattern as `buildRdEvents` — bail early on `!is_relevant_employer` / `!is_reporting_entity` / missing-deadline / out-of-horizon so the fixed disclaimer + sort ordering never see a phantom event.
  - `web/src/app/api/compliance/calendar/route.ts` — `Promise.all` grows from 3 queries to 5 (adds `compliance_wgea_status.result_json` + `compliance_modern_slavery_status.result_json`, both scoped to `(user_id, project_id)` and ordered `computed_at DESC LIMIT 1` matching the CompliancePanel loader pattern), then passes the latest `WGEAResult` / `ModernSlaveryResult` through as the new options. Zero schema churn — reuses tables `compliance_wgea_status` + `compliance_modern_slavery_status` from migration 0112 (P1n-persist). Response shape unchanged (still 200 `text/calendar; charset=utf-8` with the 5-minute private cache) so existing subscribers seamlessly pick up the two new events on next refresh.
