### Round 12 follow-ups (spun off from P11-acquisition-pattern — §1 phase 11 P1 wizard)

- **P11-acquisition-wizard** pure deal-shape checker for the 90/10 template — **shipped 2026-07-24**. Chips off the pure-helper leg of the P11-acquisition-pattern tail-note ("Founder-facing UI helper (a 'run my deal through the 90/10 checklist' wizard on `/dashboard/exit-readiness`) remains a natural next tick under P11-acquisition-wizard"). New leaf lib `web/src/lib/exits/acquisition-pattern-check.ts` exports `assessAcquisitionPattern(input) → AcquisitionPatternCheck` — scores a founder-supplied deal shape (headline value, cash %, RSU %, escrow %, escrow months, RSU cliff + vest months, target industry keywords, buyer_is_foreign_govt_influenced, buyer_is_listed, change_of_control_consents_pct) against the reusable ~90% cash / ~10% retention-RSU template Atlassian ran on Trello 2017 + OpsGenie 2018 + Loom 2023 (as codified in the P11-acquisition-pattern §1 phase-11 Chapter 11 section). Result envelope mirrors the P1i / P1n / P10-s708counter shape: `{matches_atlassian_pattern, overall_signal: "green"|"amber"|"red", cash_pct, rsu_pct, earnout_pct, escrow_pct, splits_align, rsu_structure_ok, escrow_structure_ok, firb_pre_lodgment_required, firb_reason: "threshold_exceeded"|"sensitive_sector"|"foreign_govt_influence"|null, change_of_control_gap, change_of_control_consents_pct, warnings[], recommendations[], disclaimer}`. Exported constants — `CASH_PCT_ATLASSIAN_MIN=0.85`, `CASH_PCT_ATLASSIAN_MAX=0.95`, `RSU_PCT_ATLASSIAN_MIN=0.05`, `RSU_PCT_ATLASSIAN_MAX=0.15`, `ESCROW_PCT_MIN=0.10`, `ESCROW_PCT_MAX=0.15`, `ESCROW_MONTHS_MIN=12`, `ESCROW_MONTHS_MAX=18`, `RSU_CLIFF_MONTHS=12`, `RSU_VEST_MONTHS_MIN=24`, `RSU_VEST_MONTHS_MAX=36`, `CHANGE_OF_CONTROL_CONSENT_TARGET=0.95`, `CHANGE_OF_CONTROL_CONSENT_RED_FLOOR=0.7`, `FIRB_THRESHOLD_2026_AUD=339_000_000` — kept mutable-shape point-values so a downstream UI can render the bands to the founder without hard-coding. Semantics: (a) `normalisePct` accepts either 0-1 or 0-100 inputs (`> 1` divided by 100) and clamps overshoot to 1; garbage / negative / non-finite returns `null` so the classifier can't be fed a lie. (b) `splits_align` requires cash ∈ [0.85, 0.95] AND RSU ∈ [0.05, 0.15]; `rsu_structure_ok` requires 12-month cliff + 24-36-month vest; `escrow_structure_ok` requires 10-15% held 12-18 months — a deal missing all three ranges is `matches_atlassian_pattern=false`. (c) FIRB detection: buyer_is_foreign_govt_influenced=true triggers regardless of value / sector; any of the 19 sensitive-sector keywords (critical infrastructure, energy, telecommunications, port, airport, data centre, cyber, defence, quantum, artificial intelligence, biotech, genomics, space, satellite, critical minerals, rare earth, media) case-insensitively matched against `target_industry_keywords[]` triggers regardless of value; falling below both, headline ≥ A$339M FIRB Register 2026 threshold triggers. `firb_pre_lodgment_required` never downgrades the signal to red — it's a procedural runway, not a deal-killer (matches the Chapter 11 body copy that treats FIRB as a 30-60/60-90 day runway item). (d) Overall signal: `red` when consents < 70% (`CHANGE_OF_CONTROL_CONSENT_RED_FLOOR`) OR cash > 98% (retention handcuff missing) OR cash < 50% (sellers over-exposed to buyer stock risk); `green` when the template matches AND consents on track (or unspecified); else `amber`. (e) Warnings red-first ordered — CoC gap first (60-days-to-signing pointer with the "#1 cause of post-completion price adjustments" anchor), then cash concentration, then splits gap, then RSU structure, then escrow structure, then FIRB with reason-specific runway copy. (f) Recommendations always include CGT event A1 (ITAA 1997 s104-10) timing note + Subdiv 124-M scrip-for-scrip rollover eligibility note + disclosure-schedule mapping to data-room folders 1-8 + "engage specialist M&A lawyer before signing LOI (Corrs / HSF / DLA / Gilbert + Tobin)" pointer; buyer_is_listed=true additionally recommends the Subdiv 83A-C deferred ESS check on the RSU tranche for AU-resident recipients. Fixed `ACQUISITION_PATTERN_DISCLAIMER` cites the three Atlassian precedents + the CGT / Subdiv 124-M / Subdiv 83A-C / FIRB / change-of-control considerations + the "engage a specialist M&A lawyer before signing an LOI" line so no rendering surface can leak a classification without the hedge (avoids s923B / s911A / s1041H Corps Act exposure). Test coverage `web/src/lib/exits/acquisition-pattern-check.test.ts` — 14 vitest cases across 5 describe blocks: (1) template match (textbook 90/10 SaaS deal → green + matches + Trello 2017 headline as the anchor value; 0-100 percentage normalisation; garbage / negative / non-finite tolerance), (2) red branches (consents < 70% → red + red-first warning ordering, cash > 98% → red + retention-handcuff warning, cash < 50% → red + Subdiv 124-M rollover recommendation surfaces), (3) amber branches (80/20 splits → amber + not-matches + splits-diverge warning, 85% consents → amber + soft-gap warning without the "#1 cause" anchor, 18-month RSU vest → amber + rsu_structure_ok=false + 12-month-cliff warning), (4) FIRB detection (sensitive-sector cyber keyword under A$339M → required + sensitive_sector reason + green overall since template matches; foreign_govt_influenced under A$10M → required + foreign_govt_influence reason; under-threshold + non-sensitive + non-gov → not required + null reason + no FIRB warning), (5) constants + defaults (cash-band constants + FIRB threshold exposed, disclaimer + M&A-lawyer recommendation always present). All 14 tests green (`npx vitest run src/lib/exits/acquisition-pattern-check.test.ts` — 1 file, 14 passed). Adjacent suites re-run: 44 tests green across `src/lib/exits + src/lib/investor-pack`. Typecheck `npx tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json` green (exit 0). Follows the P5-cohort / P10-s708counter-adapter / P1i shape — a pure lib shipped standalone so the sibling exit-readiness-tile UI agent has a stable contract to build against without a schema coupling. - **P10-s708counter-adapter** normalise share-register rows into the s708 counter's event shape — **shipped 2026-07-24**. Chips off the read-side of P10-s708counter-wire (still deferred as a whole because the durable share-register schema is owned by a sibling agent) by shipping a pure adapter callers can point at any share-register export today. New module `web/src/lib/compliance/s708-register-adapter.ts` exports `adaptShareRegisterRows(rows) → { events, skipped }` — a stateless normaliser that accepts a loose `ShareRegisterRow[]` (fields optional so callers can feed `equity_events` rows, an ASIC Form 484 CSV, or a founder-uploaded ROM verbatim) and returns a strict `S708OfferEvent[]` the existing `assessS708SmallScale` counter can consume unchanged plus a `skipped[]` audit trail of `(index, S708AdapterSkipReason)` pairs so a UI can surface "we couldn't parse N rows". Semantics: (a) `event_type` classifier maps `issue` / `primary` / `primary_issue` / `grant` / `allotment` → `primary` and `transfer` / `secondary` / `secondary_on_sale` / `on_sale` → `secondary_on_sale` (matches the s708 counter's `S708OfferType` union, which per s707(3) counts secondary transfers within 12 months of original issue against the same s708(1) headroom); blank `event_type` defaults to `primary`; anything else is skipped with `unknown_event_type`. (b) Exemption filter drops rows whose `exemption` tag names a non-s708(1) offer (`s708(8)` wholesale / `s708(10)` professional / `s708(11)` senior manager / `csf` / `s738G` / `prospectus` / `pds` / `ois` / `disclosure`), so a founder who has already classified their offers cannot double-count wholesale into s708(1) headroom by accident. Blank / null / `s708(1)` / `s708_1` / `s708.1` / `small_scale` / `personal_offer` all count — the counter itself is the guard-rail. (c) `investor_id` resolution walks `investor_id` → `investor_email` → normalised `investor_name` (trim + lowercase) so the same person still de-dupes across CSV columns / label conventions and the counter's `Set<investor_id>` continues to match the ASIC "issue" counting convention. (d) `amount_aud` resolution prefers explicit `amount_aud` → `consideration_aud` → `shares × price_per_share_aud`; missing all three skips with `missing_amount`, an explicit negative skips with `negative_amount` so a founder sees the bad row rather than having it silently swallowed. (e) Date normalisation accepts both `YYYY-MM-DD` and full ISO-8601 timestamps (extracting the date part), rejecting garbage with `invalid_date` distinct from `missing_date`. 11 vitest cases in `web/src/lib/compliance/s708-register-adapter.test.ts` across five describe blocks — event_type classification (primary vs secondary vs blank-default vs unknown-skip), exemption filter (non-s708 drops vs blank/null defaults to included), investor_id + amount fallbacks (id/email/name preference; missing-id vs missing-amount vs negative-amount branches; shares×price path), date normalisation (YYYY-MM-DD vs full ISO vs garbage vs missing), and an end-to-end run that feeds the adapter output straight into `assessS708SmallScale` with 19 personal-offer rows totalling A$1.8M plus a s708(8) wholesale row that must NOT eat into headroom — asserts the counter classifies `warn` on both investor-headroom and dollar-headroom guards. All 11 tests green (`npx vitest run src/lib/compliance/s708-register-adapter.test.ts` — 1 file, 11 passed). Typecheck `npx tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json` green (exit 0). Closes the read-side gap of P10-s708counter-wire so downstream cron / dashboard-panel loaders can pull rows from any source without pre-normalising. The remaining legs of P10-s708counter-wire (`/dashboard/fundraise` running-counter tile + fundraise-route pre-flight preview gate + durable share-register persistence) still depend on the sibling share-register-schema agent for the events store.

- **P11-acquisition-pattern** Chapter 11 acquisition-pattern section (§1 phase 11 P1 gap "acquisition-pattern doc showing Atlassian's ~90% cash / 10% retention template") — **shipped 2026-07-24**. Chapter 11 (`11-scale`) now carries its first `sections[]` entry `acquisition-pattern` (heading + 8-bullet body EN + VI) via the previously-declared `Chapter.sections?: ChapterSection[]` slot (web/src/lib/guide/startup-journey.ts:66). Walks the reusable ~90% cash / 10% retention-RSU template Atlassian has used on every headline post-funding acquisition end-to-end: (a) the three-precedent anchor (Trello 2017 US$425M, OpsGenie 2018 US$295M, Loom 2023 US$975M — sourced from `/showcase/atlassian` TIMELINE lines 147-155, 157-165, 183-191 + Atlassian FY18 + FY24 10-K schedules) so a founder can defend the same structure by pointing at three deals over six years, (b) why the 90/10 split works (cash gives target investors + founders a clean exit at closing; RSU keeps engineering + product leadership incentivised through the month-12→month-18 retention cliff), (c) how the RSU tranche is structured on Atlassian deals (1-year cliff + 24-36 month monthly vest, sized 2-4× annual base salary as headline value, forfeitable on separation, larger 'founder retention bonus' grant with a 4-year vest for target founders), (d) AU seller-side tax treatment where founders lose money if they don't plan the structure — CGT event A1 (ITAA 1997 s104-10) triggers on contract date not settlement date so the tax bill lands before the wire clears, Subdiv 124-M scrip-for-scrip rollover applies ONLY where consideration is shares (not cash + shares) so a 90/10 cash+RSU deal does NOT qualify for rollover on the cash tranche, RSU tranche may qualify for Subdiv 83A-C deferred ESS taxation for AU-resident employee recipients if the buyer is a listed company, (e) escrow + hold-back patterns (10-15% cash held escrow 12-18 months for indemnity claims + special indemnity escrow for known risks + FIRB approval condition-precedent for AU targets acquired by US listed buyers over the A$339M 2026 threshold or holding sensitive tech, with 30-60 days FIRB runway built into completion), (f) disclosure-schedule prep (the piece founders under-invest in — every material contract with a change-of-control clause needs to be listed with counter-party consent obtained pre-completion or completion made conditional on consent; missing consents are the #1 cause of post-completion price adjustments at 5-10% common; BlockID's data-room folders 1, 2, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8 at `data-room-templates.ts:428-1151` are structured so the schedule maps 1:1 to folder headings), (g) real-signal check — the four signals that distinguish being-acquired from pattern-matching-an-acquisition-thesis (unsolicited Corp Dev approach; competitive bake-off within 4-6 weeks; buyer's CEO/product-lead direct outreach in the first two calls; buyer asking for cohort data + eng-team org chart before financial diligence), (h) BlockID wiring — CFO exit-readiness assemble at `/dashboard/exit-readiness` (see P12b-tile) includes an 'acquisition-structure playbook' section citing the 90/10 template + AU tax + FIRB + escrow guardrails, au-compliance agent drafts the FIRB pre-lodgment questionnaire when target-industry keywords hit the sensitive-tech list, CLO agent runs a change-of-control clause scan across data-room folder 8 (Contracts & Agreements) 60 days out from any signed LOI, with an explicit "engage a specialist M&A lawyer before signing an LOI — the LOI is where 80% of the leverage is set" pointer to the AU sell-side practices at Corrs / HSF / DLA / Gilbert + Tobin. Both marketing (`/guide/11-scale`) and workspace (`/workspace/guide/11-scale`) surfaces render `sections[]` via the existing `c.sections?.map()` blocks — no page edits needed (reuses the P1j / P1l / P8-shipit wiring). - **P12c-lp-report-anon-policy** LP-report founder-slot anonymisation policy + test fixture (§1 phase 12 P2 gap "LP-report anonymisation k-threshold policy documented but no unit-test fixture") — **shipped 2026-07-24**. Closes the last outstanding §1 phase-12 P2 gap. New pure lib `web/src/lib/investor-pack/lp-report-anonymisation.ts` codifies the founder-side of the accelerator LP-report bundle — the mirror of the reseller-side `K_ANONYMITY_THRESHOLD=5` at `web/src/lib/reseller/portfolio-aggregates.ts:24` — so both ends of the pipe now agree on when a cohort is too small to expose and on which fields must be stripped from a founder-authored slot before it can be handed to the reseller side. Exports: (a) constant `LP_REPORT_K_THRESHOLD=5` explicitly asserted against the reseller-side `K_ANONYMITY_THRESHOLD` so a drift on either side breaks the test; (b) `LP_REPORT_GROWTH_BANDS` — 5-band chip pack (`contracting` / `flat` / `steady` / `strong` / `hyper`) covering the full real line so `pickGrowthBand()` never returns null on a finite MoM average; (c) fixed `LP_REPORT_ANONYMISATION_DISCLAIMER` citing Privacy Act 1988 (Cth) APP 6 (use / disclosure) + s766B Corporations Act 2001 (Cth) so no rendering surface can leak the founder slot without the AFSL + APP boundary on face; (d) `assessLpReportSlot({slot, cohortSize, peersInSameGrowthBand?, k?, revealShape?, cohortId?}) → LpReportSlotAssessment` returning `{ok, redactions[], warnings[], slot, disclaimer}` with a 5-way branch matrix pinned by the test fixture. Semantics: (1) `cohortSize < k` blanks every sensitive field except the low-risk sector + growthPhase chips and reports `cohort_below_k` (matches the guide copy at `web/src/lib/guide/startup-journey.ts:1115` "the shape of your revenue curve is recognisable to peers"), (2) `cohortSize >= k` always strips company name + founder names + customer logos (APP 6 + s18 ACL) and always coarsens exact valuation → 5-band chip (`sub_1m` / `1m_5m` / `5m_25m` / `25m_100m` / `100m_plus`) and exact headcount → 5-band chip (`solo` / `small` / `medium` / `large` / `very_large`) so a founder cannot re-identify by triangulating from the exact numbers even at k+, (3) `revealShape=false` (the safe default) buckets the monthly revenue series into a single growth-band chip via `averageMoMGrowth()` and reports `revenue_curve_shape_hidden` so peers cannot recognise the founder from the curve shape (zero + non-finite entries silently skipped in the MoM computation so a bursty ramp doesn't blow up the average), (4) `revealShape=true` exposes the raw series verbatim only when cohort meets k — the founder has explicitly opted in, (5) `peersInSameGrowthBand < k-1` warns even at cohortSize >= k because the founder is uniquely recognisable inside their (sector, growth-band) slice — the reseller must widen the band or drop the chip; warn (not block) so the founder makes the final call per Chapter 12 mentoring copy. Defensive posture: NaN / negative cohortSize coerces to 0 → cohort_below_k branch; garbage / non-finite valuation + headcount coarsen to null without throwing; malformed cohortId falls back to a generic `cohort-founder-anon` tag. Test coverage `web/src/lib/investor-pack/lp-report-anonymisation.test.ts` — 22 vitest cases across 6 describe blocks: (a) constants (k=5 asserted equal to reseller K_ANONYMITY_THRESHOLD, growth bands ordered lowest→highest MoM + cover full real line + adjacent bands share boundaries, disclaimer cites APP 6 + s766B + Corporations Act + withdraw-right), (b) cohort-below-k branch (blanks all sensitive fields + preserves non-identifying chips + cohort_below_k redaction + bespoke k override still blocks + cohortId fallback), (c) always-on strips (company + founder names + customer logos stripped every time at k+ with distinct redaction kinds, valuation coarsened to correct band with matching redaction, headcount coarsened to correct band with matching redaction, non-finite / negative inputs coarsen to null without throwing), (d) revenue-curve shape (default hides raw series + surfaces steady-band chip for +13.8% MoM ramp + revenue_curve_shape_hidden redaction; revealShape=true exposes series verbatim + omits the shape-hidden redaction; no series + no ARR → no_signal + null growthBand; ARR-only → revenue_coarsened not no_signal; hyper band for +50% MoM ramp; contracting band for -10% MoM decline; zero + non-finite entries silently skipped in MoM computation), (e) founder-isolated-in-band warning (fires when peersInSameGrowthBand < k-1 with "identify you" copy + founder_isolated_in_band redaction; skips when >= k-1; omitted entirely when field not supplied), (f) disclaimer round-trip + defensive input handling (disclaimer attached on all three branches — blocked, ok-full, empty-slot; NaN + negative cohortSize both fall into cohort_below_k). All 22 tests green; adjacent `src/lib/investor-pack` suite still green (41 tests across 4 files). Typecheck `npx tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json` green (exit 0). Follows the P5-cohort / P10-rofr-workflow / P10-s708counter-adapter shape — a pure lib shipped standalone so the `/workspace/lp-report` stub UI + the sibling IR-portfolio-bundling agent have a stable contract to build against without touching any reseller-side code (per the top-of-file constraint that this goal does NOT touch reseller code). Downstream wiring — the `/workspace/lp-report` composer needs to import `assessLpReportSlot()` + render the `redactions[]` + `warnings[]` as a "here's what will be exposed" preview before the founder confirms the bundle-lock — remains a natural next tick under P12c-lp-report-ui once the sibling workspace-shell agent picks up the stub page (`web/src/app/workspace/lp-report/page.tsx` is 31 lines of coming-soon copy today — **shipped 2026-07-24 as P12c-lp-report-ui**, see the "Last updated" tick note below and the full-body composer at `web/src/app/workspace/lp-report/lp-report-composer-client.tsx`).

Idempotent by construction: identical HTML → identical sha12 → 200 `{persisted: false, existing: true, existing_id}`; content change → different sha12 → fresh 201 row (history preserved). `dry_run: true` short-circuits persistence for founder previews. 9 vitest cases in `web/src/app/api/landing-page/publish/route.test.ts` (401 anon; 400 invalid_json; 400 invalid_body; publish_refused on invalid input; publish_refused on too-short slug; dry_run skips persistence; first publish 201 with all persisted columns pinned; re-publish identical content 200 no-op; content change produces two distinct rows) all green; `npx tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json` exit 0. Subdomain routing at `<slug>.landing.blockid.au` remains a follow-up under P4a-publish-storage; the `canonical_url` in the response is aspirational until that ships.

*Previous tick (P7a-mover-callout — founder-side weekly digest now surfaces a "Biggest mover this week" callout above the P7a-climb-delta week-over-week table, closing the magnitude-only half of the P7a-climb-delta tail-note "a 'biggest mover this week' callout summarising the single phase with the largest absolute delta ... remain a natural next tick under P7a-mover-callout". Ships as pure additions to `web/src/lib/email/founder-digest.ts` — zero schema churn, zero new files, zero changes to the founder-weekly-digest cron route (the callout reads the same `readinessByPhase` + `previousReadinessByPhase` inputs the delta table already consumes). Three edits: (a) two new exported pure helpers — `pickBiggestMover(cells) → ClimbDeltaCell | null` (walks the 12 delta cells, skips `direction === "same"`, ranks by magnitude — `Math.abs(delta)` for up/down cells, `Math.abs(currScore)` for `"new"` cells so an entering phase at 40 beats a same-band swing of +2; ties break current-phase-first then phase-ordinal-ascending so a founder standing in the tied phase always sees themselves and repeat digests stay deterministic; returns null on empty series / all-same-direction / zero-magnitude edge cases so the caller can silently drop the section) + `formatMoverCallout(cell) → {headline, hint, icon, colour}` (per-direction copy pack: up → `▲` + emerald `#047857` + "climbed +N pts to X/100" headline + "keep the workflow that moved it" hint; down → `▼` + rose `#be123c` + "slipped -N pts to X/100" headline + "check what changed in your data room since the last digest" hint — magnitude-only; the causal-attribution half stays deferred; new → `★` + sky `#0369a1` + "entered your readiness map at X/100" headline + "wasn't scored in last week's digest" hint; current-phase gets a " (you are here)" chip inline in the headline so the callout reads naturally in both HTML + text). (b) HTML render — new `moverCalloutBlock` inserted between `climbBlock` and `climbDeltaBlock` so the callout reads above the full delta table (founder eye lands on the headline first, then the table for context); fires only when `climbDeltaMoved` is true AND `pickBiggestMover(cells)` returns non-null so the section obeys the same omit rules as the delta table (fully-flat week → nothing rendered; no previous snapshot → nothing rendered); every dynamic value routes through `escapeHtml` / `escapeAttr` so a corrupted band string can't inject markup into the callout. (c) Plain-text mirror — same conditional block emits "Biggest mover this week: {icon} {headline}" + hint immediately above the "Week-over-week climb" table so plain-text clients see the same story. Test coverage `web/src/lib/email/founder-digest.test.ts` gains a "buildFounderDigest — biggest mover callout (P7a-mover-callout)" describe block — 5 vitest cases pinning (a) full render — Phase 9 (you-are-here) entering the map at 40 beats phase 3's -5 and phase 1's +2 by magnitude, callout appears above the delta table in the HTML source order via `indexOf` guard, text mirror carries the same headline; (b) omits the callout when nothing moved (mirrors the delta-table omit rule so a flat week doesn't get a spurious "biggest mover" of zero); (c) omits when previous snapshot is absent (first digest has nothing to compare against); (d) `pickBiggestMover` branch matrix — magnitude wins over sign (down -8 beats up +5), current-phase breaks ties (magnitude-5 tie between phase 2 and current phase 6 → phase 6 wins), ascending-phase-ordinal breaks ties with no current-phase in the tied set (phase 2 wins over phase 6 when the founder is standing in phase 10), flat series → null; (e) `formatMoverCallout` per-direction copy pack — up returns `▲` + `#047857` + "climbed +N pts" copy, down returns `▼` + `#be123c` + "slipped -N pts" + "you are here" chip when current, new returns `★` + `#0369a1` + "entered your readiness map at X/100" copy. All 22 tests in the file green (17 existing + 5 new). Typecheck `npx tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json` green (exit 0). *Previous tick (P5a — pure per-phase readiness helper at `web/src/lib/investor-pack/readiness-by-phase.ts` closing the last two open exit criteria on `P5_investor_readiness_score`: `readiness_by_phase(input) → Record<PhaseKey, {score, band, missing_top3}>` + reuse of the CRITERIA weights from `evaluation-criteria.ts` for a per-phase SVI subset. Ships as a leaf module — no I/O, no imports besides the `CRITERIA` fixture + the `PhaseKey`/`ALL_PHASE_KEYS` tuple from `journey-map.ts`. Exports `PHASE_CRITERIA: Record<PhaseKey, readonly CriterionKey[]>` sourced from the §1 phase-gap-matrix in this goal file (phase 1 = idea + founder_profile; phase 2 = idea + market + customer_size; phase 3 = market + customer_size + gtm_strategy; phase 4 = code_git + website + roadmap; phase 5 = customer_size + revenue + gtm_strategy + code_git; phase 6 = revenue + gtm_strategy + documents + code_git; phase 7 = revenue + gtm_strategy + team_structure + customer_size; phase 8 = team + team_structure + founder_profile + documents; phase 9 = documents + dataroom + team + revenue + founder_profile; phase 10 = dataroom + documents + revenue + team; phase 11 = revenue + team_structure + dataroom + documents; phase 12 = dataroom + documents + revenue + team). Overlap is deliberate — revenue matters from phase 5, dataroom from phase 9 — so the readiness bar rises as the raise gets bigger. `computeReadinessByPhase({criterionScores})` walks every phase, weight-sums the caller's per-criterion 0..100 scores by `CRITERIA.weight`, rounds to a 0-100 phase score, bands via `READINESS_BAND_THRESHOLDS` (ready ≥ 75, nearly-ready ≥ 45 — same thresholds `fundraise-checklist.ts::computeReadinessScore` uses so the two surfaces don't drift), and emits `missing_top3` (cap 3, only criteria under `MISSING_CRITERION_SCORE_FLOOR=60`, ordered by lost-weight × gap descending with CRITERIA-declaration-order tiebreak so the output is stable). `safeScore()` clamps to [0,100] and rejects NaN/negative/non-finite so a missing criterion doesn't accidentally count as full credit. `bandOf()` exported so downstream tiles + digests classify with the same rule. Test coverage `readiness-by-phase.test.ts` — 9 vitest cases across 5 describe blocks: fixture completeness (every 1..12 phase has ≥1 criterion + only known CriterionKey values), safeScore branch matrix (clamp + non-finite tolerance), bandOf boundary cases at both thresholds, happy-path (every phase = 100/ready when all 13 criteria maxed; every phase = 0/not-ready with empty input), missing_top3 semantics (cap at 3, floor at 60, phase-9 ordering pinned against real CRITERIA weights — team 22×0.7=15.4 > revenue 28×0.5=14.0 > founder_profile 22×0.6=13.2), and later-phase penalties (phase 10 surfaces dataroom in missing_top3 when it's 0; phase 1 hits ready with just idea + founder_profile). All 9 tests green; adjacent `src/lib/investor-pack` suite still green (50 tests across 5 files). Typecheck `npx tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json` green (exit 0). Follows the P12b / P11-acquisition-wizard / P10-rofr-workflow shape — pure lib shipped standalone so downstream consumers (the InvestorReadinessTile / the nudge engine / the weekly digest) have a stable contract to build against without a schema coupling. Dashboard tile wiring (grow the existing InvestorReadinessTile to render the per-phase Record via the sibling dashboard-shell agent) + nudge-engine wiring (compute `readiness_by_phase` inside `computeNextSteps` and expose in the `/api/nudge/next-steps` JSON so a founder can see readiness climb from Chapter 1 to Chapter 12) + weekly-digest wiring (P7a — pull the current-phase entry into the "Your investor readiness this week" section) remain natural next ticks under P5a-tile-wire / P5a-nudge-wire / P7a because they need a sibling UI / cron pass — this tick ships the pure helper so any surface that needs a per-phase readiness envelope can import it without waiting.)*

*Previous tick (P5-tax-invoice-checker — pure ATO tax-invoice format checker at `web/src/lib/compliance/tax-invoice-checker.ts` closing §1 phase 5 P1 gap "ATO tax-invoice format checker absent". Exports `assessTaxInvoice(input) → TaxInvoiceResult` classifying the invoice into `under_threshold | standard | large` band by A$82.50 + A$1,000 GST-inclusive-total thresholds (per ATO GST tax-invoice guidance under A New Tax System (Goods and Services Tax) Act 1999 (Cth) s 29-70(1)), returning `{ok, band, gst_inclusive_total_aud, computed_gst_component_aud, missing_fields[], warnings[], recommendations[], disclaimer}` with the missing-field union covering the 9 ATO-mandated slots (`gst_inclusive_total`, `supplier_name`, `supplier_abn`, `supplier_abn_invalid`, `issue_date`, `description`, `gst_amount_or_statement`, `document_label`, `recipient_abn_or_name`). Reuses `validateAbnChecksum` from P1g/abn.ts so a typo'd supplier ABN surfaces as `supplier_abn_invalid` distinct from `supplier_abn` missing. Standard-rate GST cross-check: `computed_gst_component_aud = total ÷ 11` (rounded to cents; zero for `supply_is_gst_free` / `supply_is_input_taxed` flagged supplies); declared `gst_amount_aud` outside a A$0.02 tolerance surfaces a warning rather than a missing field so a legitimate rounding edge case doesn't block a founder. Large-band (>= A$1,000) requires `recipient_name` OR `recipient_abn` per ATO; recipient ABN checksum failure downgrades to a warning (not missing) because the field IS present just wrong. GST-free / input-taxed flag mismatch (positive `gst_amount_aud` on a GST-free supply) surfaces as a warning. Fixed `TAX_INVOICE_DISCLAIMER` cites the s 29-70(1) rule + recipient-created tax invoice (s 29-70(3)) + input-taxed + GST-free + second-hand goods carve-outs + "confirm with a registered tax agent" boundary so no rendering surface can leak an OK classification without the ATO-substitute hedge. Input tolerant: non-finite / negative `gst_inclusive_total_aud` coerces to 0 → `gst_inclusive_total` missing field, blank / whitespace strings on name/description/date all fail `nonEmpty` cleanly. Test coverage `web/src/lib/compliance/tax-invoice-checker.test.ts` — 20 vitest cases across 6 describe blocks: (1) `classifyBand` (under-threshold ok+warning, zero-total → missing, A$82.50 boundary lands standard, A$999.99 stays standard while A$1,000 flips large), (2) standard-band mandatory fields (green path all-present with 330÷11=30 computed component + data-room recommendation, missing name/date/description/label surface as a set, blank vs wrong-checksum ABN split, invalid ISO date fails), (3) GST amount vs 'includes GST' statement (both missing → `gst_amount_or_statement`, statement-only sufficient, within-tolerance passes silent, outside-tolerance warns), (4) large-invoice recipient (missing both → `recipient_abn_or_name`, name-only satisfies, wrong-checksum recipient ABN warns), (5) GST-free / input-taxed (both skip the GST amount check + add labelling recommendation + zero computed component; positive GST on GST-free flags mismatch), (6) input tolerance (NaN → 0, negative → 0). All 20 tests green (`npx vitest run src/lib/compliance/tax-invoice-checker.test.ts` — 1 file, 20 passed). Full `src/lib/compliance/` suite re-run: 185 tests green across 13 files. Typecheck `npx tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json` green (exit 0). Follows the P1i / P1n / P10-s708counter / P10-rofr-workflow shape — a pure lib shipped standalone so downstream surfaces (CFO agent audit-invoice pass, `/dashboard/data-room` inline receipt validator, nightly BAS-lodgment prep cron) can consume it without a schema coupling. Founder-facing UI wizard on `/compliance/gst` or `/workspace/tax-invoice-checker`, plus a persistence table for `compliance_tax_invoice_checks` snapshots, remain natural next ticks under P5-tax-invoice-checker-ui + P5-tax-invoice-checker-persist.)*

Response shape: `{markdown, html, validation: {valid, reasons[]}}` — validation ALWAYS surfaces alongside the render so a founder sees the gap list even for a partial preview (the pure renderer emits `(headline missing)` / `_(sub-headline missing)_` / `(CTA label + href required)` placeholders for missing fields; the `validation.reasons[]` is the machine-readable pair). 400 branches: `invalid_json` on non-JSON body + `invalid_body` on non-object JSON (arrays, primitives). *Previous tick (P11-acquisition-wizard — new leaf lib `web/src/lib/exits/acquisition-pattern-check.ts` exports `assessAcquisitionPattern(input) → AcquisitionPatternCheck` that scores a founder-supplied deal shape (headline value, cash %, RSU %, escrow %, escrow months, RSU cliff + vest months, target industry keywords, buyer_is_foreign_govt_influenced, buyer_is_listed, change_of_control_consents_pct) against the reusable ~90% cash / ~10% retention-RSU template Atlassian ran on Trello 2017 + OpsGenie 2018 + Loom 2023 (as codified in the P11-acquisition-pattern §1 phase-11 Chapter 11 section). Result envelope: {matches_atlassian_pattern, overall_signal: "green"|"amber"|"red", cash_pct, rsu_pct, earnout_pct, escrow_pct, splits_align, rsu_structure_ok, escrow_structure_ok, firb_pre_lodgment_required, firb_reason: "threshold_exceeded"|"sensitive_sector"|"foreign_govt_influence"|null, change_of_control_gap, change_of_control_consents_pct, warnings[], recommendations[], disclaimer}. Percentage inputs normalised — accepts both 0-1 and 0-100; garbage/negative/non-finite returns null so the classifier can't be fed a lie. Signal branches: red when consents < 70% OR cash > 98% (no retention handcuff) OR cash < 50% (sellers over-exposed to buyer stock); green when template matches AND consents on track (or unspecified); else amber. FIRB detection triggers on buyer_is_foreign_govt_influenced OR any of 19 sensitive-sector keywords (cyber/defence/quantum/AI/biotech/genomics/space/critical infrastructure/etc.) OR headline ≥ A$339M FIRB Register 2026 threshold — never downgrades the signal to red, treated as procedural runway (matches Chapter 11 body copy). Warnings red-first ordered — CoC gap first with the "#1 cause of post-completion price adjustments" anchor, then cash concentration, then splits gap, then RSU structure, then escrow structure, then FIRB with reason-specific runway copy (30-60 vs 60-90 days). Recommendations always include CGT event A1 (ITAA 1997 s104-10) timing + Subdiv 124-M scrip-for-scrip rollover eligibility + disclosure-schedule mapping to data-room folders 1-8 + specialist M&A lawyer pointer (Corrs/HSF/DLA/Gilbert + Tobin); buyer_is_listed=true additionally recommends the Subdiv 83A-C deferred ESS check. Fixed ACQUISITION_PATTERN_DISCLAIMER cites the three Atlassian precedents + the CGT / Subdiv 124-M / Subdiv 83A-C / FIRB / change-of-control considerations + "engage a specialist M&A lawyer before signing an LOI" line so no rendering surface can leak a classification without the hedge (avoids s923B / s911A / s1041H Corps Act exposure). Tests: 14 vitest cases across 5 describe blocks — template match (textbook 90/10 → green + Trello 2017 headline; 0-100 percentage normalisation; garbage tolerance), red branches (consents < 70%, cash > 98%, cash < 50%), amber branches (80/20 splits, 85% consents, 18-month RSU vest), FIRB detection (sensitive-sector cyber keyword under A$339M, foreign_govt_influenced under A$10M, under-threshold + non-sensitive + non-gov), constants + defaults. All 14 tests green; adjacent 44 tests green across src/lib/exits + src/lib/investor-pack. Typecheck `npx tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json` green (exit 0). The founder-facing form + client wizard that mounts this into /dashboard/exit-readiness (below the exit-benchmark tile) remains a natural next tick under P11-acquisition-wizard-ui.)*

WGEA spec pins six cases — (a) headcount 120 → `action_required=report_required` + `urgency=critical` + `is_relevant_employer=true` + `is_above_threshold=true` + `threshold_headcount=100` + present `next_report_due_iso` + present `disclaimer`, (b) headcount 12 → `not_required` + `ok`, (c) headcount 88 → `approaching_threshold` + `warning` (80–99 band, `is_above_threshold=false`), (d) missing `has_previously_reported` → 400 `missing_required_fields` with `required[]` containing the field name, (e) unauthenticated request (no cookies) → 401 `unauthenticated` with disclaimer, (f) GET returns 200 with disclaimer for a logged-in caller. Modern Slavery spec pins six cases — (a) current-period A$60M + projected full-period A$120M → `statement_required` + `warning` + `is_above_threshold=true` + `is_reporting_entity=true` + `threshold_aud=100_000_000`, (b) A$2.5M → `not_required` + `ok`, (c) A$90M projected → `approaching_threshold` + `warning`, (d) missing `current_period_revenue_aud` → 400 with `required[]` containing the field, (e) unauthenticated → 401 with disclaimer, (f) GET returns 200. `npx playwright test tests/e2e/compliance/wgea.spec.ts tests/e2e/compliance/modern-slavery.spec.ts --list` green (12 tests discovered under chromium).
